the repo. I also want main and the running server on the newest
commit, ready for the next feature.

WHAT TO DO
1. Log check, read-only first: list the files in .claude-logs. For each

DONE MEANS

- Main matches GitHub, its CI result is known, and the server runs the

- No code changes in this task.

HOW TO VERIFY

RULES

- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: merge commits only, no rebase, no force push. Never hand-edit a

- A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code problem:
  rerun once and make no code changes.
- If a step fails, tell me what happened and stop.

WHEN DONE

commit main is on, the CI result of main, and anything I need to
decide.

CONTEXT
Retake is my Next.js 16 video editor (React 19, Prisma, local Supabase,
Gemini and Deepgram for AI, browser-side export with MediaRecorder,
projects stored in IndexedDB). It has grown over many PRs. I want the
whole repository reviewed as it is on main today, not just recent
changes.

GOAL
Give me your own honest architecture review of the entire codebase,
before I show you anyone else's. I want to know what a strict reviewer
would deduct marks for, what is a sound trade-off, and whether the app
could be deployed for real users without surprises in cost, security or
reliability. Success is a score out of 15 with reasons, plus a ranked
list I can act on, with evidence for every item, so I spend time only on
real problems.

WHAT TO DO
Read-only. Don't change any code, make any commit or call any paid API.
1. Review the whole structure: all folders, module boundaries, how
   responsibilities are split, the biggest files, and where one file
   does too much.
2. Review the tech choices against what the app needs: Next.js server
   actions, Gemini plus Deepgram, browser-side export, IndexedDB project
   storage, local Supabase auth, Prisma. For each, say whether it fits and
   what the trade-off is.
3. Review security and cost on every paid server action: authentication
   inside the action, rate limits, quota checks before the provider call,
   request-size and timeout limits, and what happens if the auth service
   is down.
4. Review reliability: export interruption and long videos, storage
   failures and quota, media identity, error handling, and the tests and
   CI gates.
5. Deployment readiness: if this ran for real users, what would break,
   what would cost money unexpectedly, and what is missing (secrets
   handling, logging, limits, environments)? Don't deploy anything or use
   hosted services.
6. Check that documentation and comments match the code.
7. Score the architecture out of 15. Show how you got the number: what
   each area contributed and what cost marks.

DONE MEANS
- A score out of 15 with a short breakdown.

## Architecture review of Retake on `main` (`45b6d2c`): 9.5 / 15

**What matters most:** the app is well built in the places that took effort (the pure logic, the cost tracking, CI), but **a paid AI call is only as protected as a page redirect.** No paid action checks who's calling. The guard in front of them is skipped whenever Supabase is unreachable or its env vars are missing, and there's no per-user limit. So a misconfigured deploy, or one free account, can spend without limit. Second, the uploads the app relies on (about 9–14 MB) are likely larger than a typical serverless host accepts, so transcription and Make it may break on the first real deploy.

### Score

| Area | Out of | Score | What earned or cost marks |
|---|---|---|---|
| Structure and module boundaries | 3 | 1.5 | **Earned:** the pure logic (`sequence`, `overlays`, `make-plan` maths, `projects`) sits in separate, unit-tested modules. **Cost:** the `VideoEditor` component is 2,552 lines; `planMake` is one ~510-line function; the editor is one flat folder of 115 files; ~1,340 lines of unused template scaffolding. |
| Tech choices against needs | 3 | 2.5 | **Earned:** choices fit and are documented (below). **Cost:** the upload path assumes ~14 MB request bodies, which won't fit a typical serverless host. |
| Security and cost controls | 3 | 1.0 | **Earned:** strict input-size caps, Zod validation, secrets handling, RLS check. **Cost:** no session check in any paid action, the guard fails open, no rate limit or quota, open sign-up without email confirmation, public contact form writes to the DB. |
| Reliability | 3 | 2.0 | **Earned:** export length measured and warned about, empty export caught, AI errors in plain words, Make it timeouts. **Cost:** IndexedDB saves can report "Saved" before the write commits, no timeout on Deepgram or editor Gemini calls, a hidden tab during export goes undetected. |
| Tests, CI and docs | 3 | 2.5 | **Earned:** a strong gate (lint, types, format, env, secrets, standards, unit, build, migrations plus RLS, AI model check, commit messages, 4 e2e shards). **Cost:** auth never runs in CI, `retries: 2` hides flakes, the README omits the core architecture, one wrong comment. |

### Ranked findings

1. **Paid actions don't check the session, and the only guard fails open.**
   - **Evidence:**
     - `editor/actions.ts:118` and the other three editor actions, plus `make/actions.ts:180, 232, 295`, call `validatedAction({ input, handler })` with no `authorize` and no `currentUser()`.
     - `validated-action.ts:32` only authorizes when `authorize` is set.
     - The only protection is `proxy.ts`: `signedIn = … 'AuthRetryableFetchError' ? null` (line 55), then `if (signedIn === null) return null` (line 86). It also skips the guard entirely when the Supabase env vars are unset (line 23), and those are `.optional()` in `env.ts:19-20`.
     - `docs/rules/auth.md:58-66` says to resolve the session in the action and reject if absent.
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** if Supabase goes down, or a production deploy forgets two variables, all six paid endpoints are open to anyone, silently.
   - **Smallest fix:** a `requireUser()` call at the top of each paid handler that throws a 401 `AppError`, plus making the Supabase vars required in `env.ts` when `NODE_ENV=production`.
   - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure.

2. **No rate limit or per-user quota before any provider call, and cost can't be attributed to a user.**
   - **Evidence:**
     - No rate limiting anywhere: a search for `rate.?limit|quota|throttle` finds only how a provider's 429 is reported (`ai-failure.ts:24`).
     - `ai_usage` has no user column (`prisma/schema/ai-usage.prisma:12-59`).
     - `supabase/config.toml:177, 227`: `enable_signup = true`, `enable_confirmations = false`.
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** one throwaway account can loop `planMakeIt` (up to 150 images a call) or 5-minute transcriptions with no ceiling.
   - **Smallest fix:** add a nullable `user_id` to `ai_usage`, then a daily per-user cap checked before the call. Check MOAD first, because that column contract is shared with it. Also turn on email confirmation.
   - **Size:** medium. **Confidence:** sure.

3. **Request bodies of about 9–14 MB won't fit a typical serverless host.**
   - **Evidence:**
     - `next.config.ts:30-33` raises `bodySizeLimit` to `TRANSCRIBE_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES`, about 13.8 MB, for 5-minute base64 WAV (`take-audio.ts:21-35`).
     - Make it frames are allowed up to 150 × 60 KB, about 9 MB (`make/actions.ts:30, 308-312`).
     - The docs target Vercel (`docs/CI_CD_SETUP.md:103`).
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** on Vercel, as far as I know, request bodies are capped at 4.5 MB, so transcription of takes over roughly 1.5 minutes and most Make it plans would fail in production only. The limit is also global, so every action (contact, login) accepts 13.8 MB.
   - **Smallest fix:** send compressed audio (Opus is about 20× smaller than WAV) and fewer or smaller frames, or upload to storage and pass a reference. Keep the action limit at the default.
   - **Size:** medium. **Confidence:** needs checking (I can't verify hosted limits).

4. **No timeout on Deepgram or the editor's Gemini calls.**
   - **Evidence:** the `fetch` in `deepgram.ts:67` has no `signal`. `parseEditCommand` and `findHighlights` pass no `timeoutMs` (`editor/actions.ts:118, 165`), unlike the Make it actions. No `maxDuration` is set anywhere.
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** a hung provider holds a paid function until the platform kills it.
   - **Smallest fix:** add `AbortSignal.timeout(…)` to the Deepgram call and `timeoutMs` to both editor calls.
   - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure for the code; the Gemini SDK's own default timeout needs checking.

5. **IndexedDB saves resolve before the transaction commits.**
   - **Evidence:**
     - `projects.ts:88-93`: `request()` resolves on `onsuccess` and never waits for `oncomplete` or `onabort`.
     - `saveProject` (lines 151-212) writes media and the project record in separate transactions.
     - There's no `QuotaExceededError` handling.
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** a write the browser aborts at commit (over quota with large videos) could still show "Saved". A failure part-way leaves orphaned media.
   - **Smallest fix:** resolve on `tx.oncomplete`, reject on `tx.onabort` with `tx.error`, and give a clear "browser storage is full" message.
   - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure for the code; whether Chrome reports quota at the request or at commit needs checking.

6. **God files, against the repo's own rule.**
   - **Evidence:** `video-editor.tsx` has 2,845 lines, of which the component is lines 216-2768, with 88 inner functions and 81 imports. `planMake` runs from `make-plan.ts:928` to 1437. `docs/rules/code-quality.md:35-46` says split by concern, with a soft target of 300 lines.
   - **Why a reviewer cares:** this is the first thing a strict reviewer deducts for, and it makes every editor change riskier.
   - **Smallest fix:** pull out hooks (project save/open, export flow, command dispatch) whenever you next touch those areas.
   - **Size:** large. **Confidence:** sure.

7. **The public contact form writes to the database with no throttle.**
   - **Evidence:** `contact/actions.ts:8-15`.
   - **Smallest fix:** a per-IP rate limit and a honeypot field.
   - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure.

8. **Auth is never tested in CI.**
   - **Evidence:** the CI e2e job has no Supabase env, so `site.spec.ts:193` skips itself. No test proves a signed-out call to a paid action is refused.
   - **Smallest fix:** a unit test for `requireUser` in the actions (once finding 1 is done), or local Supabase in the e2e job.
   - **Size:** medium. **Confidence:** sure.

9. **Possible open redirect after sign-in.**
   - **Evidence:** `login-form.tsx:21` blocks `//…` but not `/\evil.com`, which browsers treat as `//evil.com`.
   - **Smallest fix:** accept `next` only if `new URL(next, location.origin).origin === location.origin`.
   - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** needs checking.

10. **A hidden tab during export goes undetected.**
    - **Evidence:** `export-video.ts:31` admits "hidden tabs stop drawing frames", and nothing listens for `visibilitychange`. The user is only told in a hint (`en.json:509`).
    - **Smallest fix:** abort the export with a clear message when the tab is hidden.
    - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure there's no handler; the exact effect needs checking.

11. **Docs and comments drift.**
    - **Evidence:** the README never mentions Gemini, Deepgram, IndexedDB, browser export or Make it. The comment at `projects.ts:7-8` says "nothing leaves it, as nothing else in the editor does", which is false.
    - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure.

12. **Unused template scaffolding.**
    - **Evidence:** RBAC, feature flags and admin code total about 1,342 lines. The flag registry holds only template examples (`flags.ts:85`: `new_dashboard`, `payments_module`), `admin/` has only `layout.tsx`, and nothing in the product reads a flag. The example `User` model is used only by `provisionUser`.
    - **Smallest fix:** document it as template-standard, or remove it if `check:standards` allows.
    - **Size:** medium. **Confidence:** needs checking.

13. **Weak media identity when re-linking.**
    - **Evidence:** a stand-in file only has to match name and size (`projects.ts:66-67`).
    - **Size:** small. **Confidence:** sure; the impact is low.

### Sound choices and trade-offs that just need documenting

- **Browser-side export with MediaRecorder:** no upload, no 30 MB ffmpeg.wasm, private. It costs real-time speed and needs the tab in front. This is documented, its length is measured, and an empty export is caught.
- **IndexedDB projects:** zero server storage cost and private, but per-browser only. Deleting a project garbage-collects its media.
- **AI wrapper chokepoint:** all AI calls go through two wrappers, ESLint enforces it, and cost is recorded at write time. Recording never blocks the call, and a missing price is stored as NULL, never a zero.
- **Gemini Flash-Lite:** JSON-schema output at temperature 0, terse prompts, bad replies treated as "unsupported", medium image resolution.
- **Deepgram nova-3:** chosen for timed filler words.
- **Make it inputs:** every input is size-capped, with timeouts, plus a cheap second check.
- **Data layer:** Prisma only, RLS on with no policies, proved in CI. Secrets go through `env.ts` with a scanner. `validatedAction` never throws across the server/client boundary.
- **5-minute caps:** transcription and Make it stop at 5 minutes, with a plain message.
- **User-facing honesty:** the Privacy page and FAQ accurately list what goes to Deepgram and Gemini.

### Could this be deployed for real users?

**No, not as it is. With three changes, yes, for a limited beta:**
1. Fail-closed auth inside every paid action, with the Supabase vars required in production.
2. Per-user quota plus email confirmation.
3. An upload path that fits the host's body limit, plus provider timeouts.

### What to do first, and what to leave

**First:** findings 1, 2 and 3. Fold the timeouts (4) into 1, since they're the same small change to the same files.

**Leave for now:** the god-file refactor (do it bit by bit when touching that code), the scaffolding, media identity, and `retries: 2`.

**Not checked:**
- the actual Vercel limits (no hosted services);
- the Gemini SDK's default timeout;
- whether a server action can be invoked by posting to a page that doesn't import it;
- Chrome's IndexedDB quota timing;
- export memory use on long videos.

I can also turn this review into a shareable page if you'd like one.

CONTEXT
A review of the whole repo found three reliability gaps:
- projects.ts:88-93: request() resolves on onsuccess and never waits
  for oncomplete or onabort. saveProject (lines 151-212) writes media
  and the project record in separate transactions, and there's no
  QuotaExceededError handling, so "Saved" can show before the write
  commits and a partial failure can leave orphaned media.
- export-video.ts:31 admits hidden tabs stop drawing frames, but nothing
  listens for visibilitychange. The user only gets a hint (en.json:509).
- Re-linking media matches only on name and size (projects.ts:66-67).

GOAL
Retake keeps people's work and exports, so it must never say "Saved"
when it isn't, never produce a broken export without saying so, and
never mistake one file for another. Success is that a failed or
over-quota save shows a clear error, a hidden tab during export stops
it with a clear message, and re-linking matches the right file.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- A save is successful only after its transaction commits.
- Storage-full shows a plain message ("Your browser's storage is full.
  Delete a project or free some space, then save again.") and leaves no
  orphaned media.
- A hidden tab during export aborts it with a clear message. It
  doesn't silently continue.
- Media identity uses a stronger fingerprint than name and size. Ask me
  which one if the options differ in cost.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. IndexedDB: resolve on tx.oncomplete and reject on tx.onabort with
   tx.error. Make the media and project writes consistent, or clean up
   media on failure. Handle QuotaExceededError.
2. Export: listen for visibilitychange, abort when hidden, and show the
   message. Make sure cancellation leaves no partial file.
3. Media identity: add a stronger fingerprint (for example a hash of
   the first and last part of the file plus size), and keep old
   saved projects working.

OUT OF SCOPE
Auth, rate limits, uploads, big-file splits, CI files, new features.

DONE MEANS
- Unit tests: a commit failure and a quota error both show an error
  and leave nothing orphaned; two different files with the same name
  and size are not matched; old projects still re-link.
- An end-to-end test: hiding the tab during export aborts with the
  message and offers no file.
- Tests that fail when each fix is removed.
- All existing tests and CI pass, with no check loosened. CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the test output. Tell me what you couldn't test (real browser
quota behaviour, Safari and Firefox) instead of claiming it.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

CONTEXT
Main is at 3c88c69 (includes #31). An architecture review of the whole
repo found that the paid AI actions have no session check of their own.
The only guard is proxy.ts, which skips the check when Supabase is
unreachable or its env vars are missing (proxy.ts:23, 55, 86;
env.ts:19-20). Affected: the four editor actions (editor/actions.ts) and
the Make it actions (make/actions.ts:180, 232, 295). The Deepgram call
(deepgram.ts:67) and the editor's Gemini calls (editor/actions.ts:118,
165) have no timeout. docs/rules/auth.md:58-66 says to resolve the
session inside the action and reject if absent. CI's e2e job runs
without Supabase env.

GOAL
Retake spends real money on every Gemini and Deepgram call, so a paid
action must refuse a caller who isn't signed in, whatever happens to the
page redirect or the auth service. Success is that every paid action
rejects a signed-out caller on its own, a missing Supabase setup in
production stops the app instead of opening it, and a hung provider
can't hold a call open.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- Fail closed: if the session can't be confirmed, the action refuses.
- Make the Supabase env vars required when NODE_ENV=production.
- Follow docs/rules/auth.md for how the session is resolved.
- Timeouts are added to the same files in the same PR.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Add a requireUser() check (401 AppError) at the start of every paid
   action, using the existing validatedAction authorize option where it
   fits.
2. Make the Supabase env vars required in production in env.ts.
3. Add AbortSignal.timeout to the Deepgram call, and timeoutMs to the
   editor Gemini calls, matching the Make it actions.
4. CI and tests run without Supabase. Before choosing how tests sign
   in, ask me, and recommend an option that does not weaken production
   behaviour.

OUT OF SCOPE
Rate limits, quotas, the ai_usage table, email confirmation, upload
size, splitting big files, CI files.

DONE MEANS
- A signed-out call to each paid action is refused (unit tests for each).
- Production without the Supabase vars fails to start, with a clear
  message.
- Timeout tests for Deepgram and the editor Gemini calls.
- Tests that fail when each fix is removed.
- All existing tests and CI pass, with no check loosened.
- CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the test output for the signed-out cases. Also try posting directly
to one paid action without a session, and tell me what happened. Say what
you didn't check.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide

Every paid AI action now refuses a caller who isn't signed in on its own. CI is green: run 38084213740 passed all nine jobs, and the Claude PR review passed. PR: https://github.com/8xplays/t1-alishbasaboor/pull/32

**What changed:**
- **Sign-in check:** all seven paid actions (four in the editor, three in Make it) check the session themselves and fail closed with a 401. That covers no session, Supabase being down, and Supabase not configured.
- **Production start-up:** a production server without the two Supabase settings refuses to start and logs exactly which ones to set. The build doesn't need them, so CI's build is unaffected.
- **Time limits:** Deepgram calls get 60 s for a take and 30 s for a voice command. The editor's Gemini calls get 30 s for a command and 60 s for highlights.
- **Bug found while testing:** the shared timeout helper aborted the call before reporting the timeout. A provider that fails immediately on abort won that race, and the user saw "Something went wrong" instead of the timeout message. It now reports the timeout first.
- **Test loader fixes:** tests mock modules with Node's built-in tool, as you chose, with no test bypass in the app. To make that run I fixed three things in the test loader. The `@/` path alias was broken on Windows, `server-only` now loads as it does on the server, and `next/navigation` couldn't be found.

**The call by hand:** with no cookie, the page redirect stops it (307 to `/login`). Posting it to another page gives "Server action not found". Then I made up a session cookie and briefly stopped the local auth container, then started it again. The redirect let the request through, as before, and the action itself answered `{"ok":false,"error":"Sign in to use this.","status":401}`. No usage row was written, so no provider was called. Before this PR, that request would have run the paid call.

**Tested:**
- **Signed-out cases:** all 7 paid actions refuse in all 5 signed-out situations and reach their provider when signed in. All 4 timeouts are given up at their limit.
- **Each fix is needed:** I removed each fix in turn and the matching tests failed (7 checks).
- **Local gate:** 510 unit tests pass. Full e2e passed 120/120 with accounts on. With accounts off, as CI runs it, 3 real-time recording tests failed once, touched no server action, and passed on rerun; they're recorded in the notes.
- **Mock coverage:** I listed in the PR which e2e mock answers each paid action.

**Not tested:** real Supabase sign-in in the unit tests or in CI, which runs with accounts off; local e2e does sign in for real. The real paid part of the `ai-usage-check` skill wasn't run, since it costs money; its free model check passed.

**Decisions for you:**
1. **Missing settings in production:** Next keeps the process running and answers every request with a 500 instead of exiting. Do you want a forced exit, so a host sees a crash instead?
2. **Port 3000 is empty:** I stopped the server there to run the accounts-off e2e. Shall I rebuild it from `main` once you merge #32?

CONTEXT
Main includes #32 and #33. An architecture review of the whole repo
found two small abuse risks:
- The public contact form writes to the database with no throttle
  (contact/actions.ts:8-15), so anyone can fill the table.
- login-form.tsx:21 blocks "//..." in the next parameter but not
  "/\evil.com", which browsers treat as "//evil.com". After sign-in the
  user could be sent to another site.

GOAL
A public form and a sign-in redirect are the first things an attacker
tries. Success is that the contact form can't be used to flood the
database, and sign-in can only ever send the user to a page on Retake.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- Redirect: accept the next parameter only if new URL(next,
  location.origin).origin equals location.origin and it starts with a
  single "/". Anything else goes to the default page.
- Contact form: add a honeypot field and a per-IP limit. Before choosing
  where the limit's counter lives (memory, database or something else),
  ask me, and recommend the option that survives a restart and works on
  more than one server instance without a new paid service.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Fix the redirect check as above, in every place the next parameter
   is used (check for others, not only login-form.tsx).
2. Add the honeypot and per-IP limit to the contact form, with a plain
   message when it triggers, and no hint to a bot about which check
   caught it.
3. Make sure the contact form still works for a normal user, including
   the existing e2e test.

OUT OF SCOPE
Per-user rate limits and quotas on AI actions, the ai_usage table, email
confirmation, upload size, splitting big files, CI files, Supabase
config.

DONE MEANS
- Unit tests for the redirect: normal paths pass; "//evil.com",
  "/\evil.com", "https://evil.com" and a protocol-relative URL all fall
  back to the default page.
- Tests for the contact form: a normal submit works, a filled honeypot
  is rejected, the limit blocks after the allowed number from one IP and
  allows again after the window.
- Tests that fail when each fix is removed.
- All existing tests and CI pass, with no check loosened. CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the test output. Try the redirect by hand against the running
server with a hostile next value, and try submitting the contact form
past the limit. Say what you didn't check (for example how the real
client IP appears behind a proxy or host).

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

The contact form can no longer flood the database, and sign-in only ever goes back to a page on Retake. CI is green on the PR's final commit `653fa78` (CI's own migration commit) after you approved it: all nine CI jobs and the Claude review passed. My commit `ad3a518` was green before that. PR: https://github.com/8xplays/t1-alishbasaboor/pull/34

**What changed:**
- **Redirect:** `next` is now used only if it starts with a single `/` and stays on this site; anything else goes to `/home`. That was the only place it's read.
- **Honeypot:** the contact form has a hidden field that a person never sees and a bot fills in.
- **Per-sender limit:** 5 messages an hour per sender, 30 an hour shared when a sender can't be told apart. The limit is counted from the saved messages, so it survives restarts and works on any number of servers.
- **No raw IP:** a sender is stored only as a hash of their IP made with a secret key, never the IP itself, and refusals are logged without it. Both checks give the same plain refusal.
- **Privacy page:** it now mentions the hashed address, and its date is updated.
- **Migration:** CI generated it as you required: exactly one new nullable column and one index, nothing destructive.

**Which IP header:** the last entry of `x-forwarded-for`, the one your host or proxy writes. Vercel replaces it with the real client IP, and a client can only add entries in front of it.

**What you must set when deploying:** `CONTACT_IP_SECRET`, a random value of at least 32 characters. Create it with `openssl rand -hex 32` and keep it the same across deploys, since changing it resets every sender's count. Without it, a production server logs which setting is missing and exits.

**Tested by hand** on a production build with a throwaway database, dropped afterwards:
- One sender's 6th and 7th messages were refused, another sender went through, and a filled hidden field got the same refusal. No IP appears in the table or the server log.
- Signing in through the real page with `//evil.com`, `/\evil.com`, `https://evil.com`, or `/` + tab + `/evil.com` as `next` always landed on `/home`.

**Tested automatically:** 521 unit tests pass. I removed each of the six fixes in turn and the matching tests failed every time. A new e2e test checks the hidden field and the refusal message, and the existing contact test passes unchanged.

**Not tested:**
- How a real host passes the client's IP. I relied on Vercel's documented behaviour.
- Self-hosted with no proxy in front, a client can set the IP header itself and dodge the per-sender limit.
- A burst of simultaneous messages from one sender can get a few past the limit.

**Decisions for you:**
1. Once you merge #34, run `pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy` locally, or I can. Your local database is two migrations behind, and the contact form will fail locally until then.
2. Port 3000 is empty: I stopped the `main` server for the hand tests. Shall I rebuild it from `main` after the merge?

## Status since the architecture review

**Fixed and merged**

| Finding from the review | Fixed by | What it does now |
|---|---|---|
| Paid AI actions had no session check of their own, and the proxy fails open | **#32** | All seven paid actions (four in the editor, three in Make it) check the session themselves and refuse with a 401 when they can't confirm it: no session, a bad one, Supabase unreachable, or accounts not configured. Shown by hand: with the auth service down and a made-up session cookie, the redirect let the request through and the action refused it. |
| Supabase settings optional in production | **#32, #33** | A production server missing them logs which ones to set and exits with code 1. Never during the build, in development or in tests. |
| No timeout on Deepgram or the editor's Gemini calls | **#32** | Deepgram gets 60 s for a take and 30 s for a voice command. The editor's Gemini calls get 30 s for a command and 60 s for highlights. A shared timeout race that could hide a timeout behind a generic error is fixed. |
| A save could show "Saved" before it committed, with orphaned media on failure | **#31** | Each save is one transaction that's done only once it commits. Storage full shows a plain message and leaves nothing behind. |
| A hidden tab during export went undetected | **#31** | Hiding the tab stops the export with a message and offers no file. |
| Re-linking matched files on name and size only | **#31** | Re-linking checks a fingerprint of the file's first and last megabyte plus its size. Projects saved before this still re-link on name and size. |
| The public contact form had no throttle | **#34** | A hidden honeypot field, plus a per-sender limit: 5 an hour by a keyed hash of the IP, 30 an hour shared when the sender is unknown. No raw IP is stored or logged. Production needs `CONTACT_IP_SECRET`. |
| Possible open redirect after sign-in (`/\evil.com`) | **#34** | `next` is used only if it starts with a single `/` and stays on this site; anything else goes to `/home`. Checked by hand with hostile values. |
| Wrong comment in `projects.ts` ("nothing leaves") | **#31** | Corrected. |

**Known limits still open**, as you listed:
- **No per-user rate limit or quota on AI actions.** A signed-in account can still spend without a ceiling, and `ai_usage` has no user column to count against.
- **Email confirmation is off** (`supabase/config.toml`), so accounts are free to create.
- **Uploads of about 9–14 MB** (5-minute WAV transcription, Make it's frames) may exceed a host's request-body limit, such as Vercel's 4.5 MB.
- **Export runs in real time in the browser**, so it takes as long as the edit and needs the tab in front. It's now stopped cleanly when the tab is hidden.
- **Projects are per-browser** (IndexedDB), so they don't follow the user to another browser or device.
- **Large files:** the `VideoEditor` component is about 2,550 lines and `planMake` about 510.

**Also still open from the review**, not in your list:
- **The README** still doesn't mention Gemini, Deepgram, IndexedDB, browser export or Make it.
- **Real sign-in isn't exercised in CI.** CI runs with accounts off; the signed-out refusals are covered by unit tests with module mocks, and local e2e signs in for real.
- **Unused template scaffolding** (RBAC, feature flags, the admin layout, about 1,340 lines) is still there.
- **Self-hosted without a proxy**, a client can set its own IP header and dodge the contact limit. Fine on Vercel.

**If re-scored:** I'd put it at roughly **11/15**, up from 9.5.
- Security and cost go up about 1 point. Fail-closed auth, timeouts, the boot check, the contact limit and the safe redirect are done, but there's still no AI quota and email confirmation is off.
- Reliability goes up about 0.5. Saves, hidden-tab export and re-linking are fixed; upload size and real-time export remain.
- Structure, tech choices, and tests/docs are unchanged.

CONTEXT
Main is at b9408f8 (includes #31 to #34). An architecture review found
no per-user rate limit or quota on the seven paid AI actions (four in
the editor, three in Make it). Sign-up is open and email confirmation
is off, so one account can spend without a ceiling. ai_usage has no user
column, and it's shared with MOAD, so I don't want to change it.

GOAL
Retake spends real money on every Gemini and Deepgram call. Success is
that each signed-in user has a daily ceiling, checked before the
provider is called, so a single account can't run up an unbounded bill.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- Don't touch ai_usage. Count in a new table of my own, keyed by user
  and day.
- The check and the count must be atomic, so simultaneous requests
  can't go past the ceiling.
- The check happens before the provider call, after the sign-in check
  from #32.
- When the ceiling is reached, show a plain message that says when it
  resets, and call no provider.
- The migration is additive only (a new table). Follow the repo's
  migration rule exactly and tell me what the check expects before you
  generate it.
- Before choosing what to count (calls, cost, or both) and the daily
  numbers, ask me, and recommend values that cover a normal day of
  editing (including a 5-minute transcription and a Make it plan) while
  stopping a loop.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Add the table and the check-and-count function.
2. Call it in all seven paid actions, after sign-in and before the
   provider.
3. Make sure a failed or timed-out provider call doesn't permanently
   use up the user's allowance unfairly. Tell me what you chose.
4. Document the limit and its numbers where the users can see them (the
   FAQ or Privacy page, if the app lists limits there).

OUT OF SCOPE
Email confirmation, Supabase config, ai_usage, upload size, splitting
big files, CI files.

DONE MEANS
- Unit tests for each action: under the ceiling it reaches the fake
  provider and counts; at the ceiling it refuses with the plain message
  and the provider is not called; the next day it works again.
- A test that simultaneous requests can't exceed the ceiling.
- Tests that fail when each fix is removed.
- All existing tests and CI pass, with no check loosened. CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the test output. Try it by hand against the running server on a
throwaway database until the ceiling refuses, and show that no provider
was called. Say what you didn't check.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=20 session=b42658ff-19b2-4aa3-84fd-8e0832d6120c]
timestamp: 2026-10-10T23:43:45.013Z

Cause confirmed in Next's own env loader (`@next/env@16.3.0`): it **deletes** any variable whose value is empty (`if(e[t]===undefined||e[t]===""){delete process.env[t]}`) and then loads the value from `.env.local`. So `GEMINI_API_KEY=''` became your real key. I assumed an empty value would block it and didn't check before the call.

**What happened, exactly:**
- **One real Gemini call:** `parse_edit_command`, 878 input and 153 output tokens, status `ok`, **646 micro-USD (about $0.0006)**, at 23:42:57 UTC. It's recorded in the throwaway database's `ai_usage`, which isn't your real one.
- **No other paid call:** calls 2 and 3 were refused by the ceiling before any provider call (the `ai_usage` count stayed at 1), and nothing called Deepgram. The server is stopped.
- **The allowance did its job:**
  - At 299 used, the call went through and the count reached 300.
  - At 300, both calls were refused with "You've used today's 300 typed edit commands. They reset at midnight UTC, in 18 min." (it was 23:42 UTC).
  - No provider call was made at the ceiling.

**Nothing is committed or pushed yet.** The code, tests and migration work are done and verified locally. Remaining:
- the PROGRESS_NOTES update;
- dropping the throwaway database;
- the commit, PR and CI, plus the RLS commit after CI generates the table migration;
- restoring the `main` server on port 3000, which I stopped to regenerate the Prisma client.

None of that calls an AI provider.

How do you want me to proceed?
1. **Continue (my recommendation):** finish the PR as planned, with this unplanned call stated plainly in the PR and in PROGRESS_NOTES, and no further hand tests that could reach a provider.
2. **Stop here** while you review what happened.

For any future hand test like this, I'd remove the key from the server's environment entirely, by running from a copy without `.env.local` or overriding it with an invalid non-empty value, and then confirm the key is absent before making any call.

The daily AI allowance is done in PR #35 (https://github.com/8xplays/t1-alishbasaboor/pull/35), and the second CI run is fully green, so it's ready to merge.

**What changed:**
- **Allowance:** every paid AI action now takes one of the user's daily allowance, after the sign-in check and before any provider is called.
- **Limits:** typed commands 300, spoken commands 200, transcripts 30, highlight searches 30, Make it 60. They live in one place, `DAILY_AI_LIMITS` in `src/lib/ai-limits.ts`. Changing them is a normal code change (PR, CI, merge, deploy) and takes effect on the same day's counts. The Pricing page reads the same numbers.
- **Counting:** in a new table of yours, `ai_allowances`, so `ai_usage` is untouched. Each take is one atomic update.
- **At the ceiling:** a plain message says when it resets ("…They reset at midnight UTC, in 14 h 0 min."), and no provider is called.
- **Fairness:** a call whose provider fails or times out is given back.

**Worst case at these numbers:** about **$4.58 a day per account, so about $458 a day for 100 accounts**. Sign-up is open and email confirmation is off, so that's the exposure before a public deploy. It's in the PR.

**The accidental call:** it's stated in the PR and in PROGRESS_NOTES: one `parse_edit_command` call to Gemini, about $0.0006, caused by Next's env loader replacing my empty `GEMINI_API_KEY` with the real key from `.env.local`. It was recorded in the throwaway database, so your real `ai_usage` doesn't include it. "Hand tests never reach a provider" is now a standing rule in the notes.

**Tested:**
- **Unit:** each of the seven actions is counted under its ceiling, refused at it with no provider call, and works again the next day. 50 simultaneous requests with 10 left take exactly 10, and failed or timed-out calls are given back. I removed each of the 7 fixes in turn and the matching tests failed every time, including a check-then-count version that only the simultaneous-requests test catches.
- **Real Postgres, by hand:** 400 simultaneous requests took exactly 300.
- **e2e:** 121/121 against the branch with invalid AI keys. Your real `ai_usage` stayed at 374 rows throughout.

**Not tested:** real Postgres atomicity in CI (the unit tests use an in-memory stand-in), real Supabase sign-in in CI, and behaviour under a real host's load.

**CI:**
- The first run was red on `check:rls` only, as expected, with `ai_allowances` the only table named.
- My RLS commit (`ef001a9`, content shown before writing) made the second run fully green: all nine jobs, including `check:rls`, plus the Claude review.
- CI's own migration commit (`52fbd07`) has two runs held at "action required". They're not the PR's head and don't block it. Approve them only if you want them run.

**Also done:** I dropped the throwaway database, and your real database's row counts are unchanged. `main` is back on port 3000 (HTTP 200), served from a separate exported copy so branch builds can't disturb it.

**For you to decide:**
1. **Merge #35.** After that, run `pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy` locally to create the table. Until then, a locally run build of this branch would fail paid actions with a missing-table error.
2. **The numbers:** are the daily limits and the $4.58-per-account worst case acceptable before a public deploy? If not, lower Make it first, since it's about 80% of the worst case.
3. **The bot commit's held runs:** approve or leave them. Either is fine for the PR.

CONTEXT
Main includes #31 to #35. An architecture review of the whole repo
found the docs lag the code. The README never mentions Gemini,
Deepgram, IndexedDB, browser export or Make it. video-editor.tsx has a
comment saying project persistence is still to be wired up, though
projects.ts already does it. Since the review, these are merged:
- #31 save and export reliability: saves commit before "Saved", storage
  full is handled, a hidden tab stops the export, re-linking uses a
  file fingerprint.
- #32 and #33 fail-closed sign-in check inside all seven paid actions,
  provider timeouts, and a production boot check for the Supabase
  settings.
- #34 contact form honeypot and per-sender limit (keyed IP hash, needs
  CONTACT_IP_SECRET in production), and a safe sign-in redirect.
- #35 per-user daily limits per AI action, in DAILY_AI_LIMITS
  (src/lib/ai-limits.ts), counted in the ai_allowances table.
Known limits still open: email confirmation is off, uploads of 9-14 MB
may exceed a host's request-body limit, export runs in real time in the
browser, projects are per-browser, the VideoEditor component (about
2,550 lines) and planMake (about 510) are large, the unused template
scaffolding (about 1,340 lines) is still there, and CI doesn't exercise
real sign-in.

GOAL
A reviewer should understand how Retake is built, why each choice was
made and what its limits are in five minutes, without reading the
code. Success is a README and an ARCHITECTURE.md that are accurate for
main today, explain the trade-offs honestly, and no comment that
contradicts the code.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- Describe only what main does today. Don't claim anything planned.
- State the weaknesses plainly in a "Known limits" section, using the
  list above, with what I would do next.
- Include a Mermaid diagram (it renders on GitHub).
- Don't put any score or rating in the docs.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. README: what Retake is, the stack, a short architecture overview,
   how to run it locally (Docker and local Supabase), the required
   settings for production (including CONTACT_IP_SECRET and how to
   create it), how to run the tests, and what CI checks.
2. ARCHITECTURE.md with:
   - a Mermaid diagram of how a video moves through the app: what stays
     in the browser, what goes to Deepgram and Gemini, how the sign-in
     check, daily limits and logging of cost sit in front of the AI
     calls, and how export and project storage work;
   - a module map: sequence.ts, transitions.ts, transform.ts,
     audio-lane.ts, edit-command.ts, export-video.ts, projects.ts, the
     AI wrappers (google.ts, deepgram.ts), validated-action.ts,
     ai-limits.ts and make-plan.ts;
   - key decisions with the reason and the trade-off: Gemini for command
     parsing, Deepgram added after measuring Gemini timestamps 250-700
     ms off, gap detection for filler cuts, browser-side export,
     IndexedDB projects, local Supabase, the AI wrapper chokepoint with
     cost tracking, the 5-minute caps, fail-closed sign-in, daily
     limits in their own table, the contact form limit;
   - known limits and deployment notes;
   - what I would do next.
3. Fix the stale persistence comment in video-editor.tsx and any other
   comment that contradicts the code.

OUT OF SCOPE
Any code change besides comments. No refactors, no CI files, no new
features.

DONE MEANS
- Every claim in the docs is true of main today, checked against the
  code.
- The diagram renders on GitHub.
- The stale comments are fixed.
- All existing checks pass, including formatting.
- CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
For each decision in ARCHITECTURE.md, name the file that proves it. List
anything you were unsure of and left out.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE writing. One clear question at a
  time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: no code changes except comments.
- Tests: no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a test.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what you couldn't confirm, the
CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

CONTEXT
Main is at 4090b60 (includes #36). An architecture review found the
editor is one flat folder of 115 files (src/app/[locale]/editor/),
mixing pure logic (sequence.ts, transitions.ts, transform.ts,
audio-lane.ts, edit-command.ts), export, project storage (projects.ts),
overlays, UI components and tests. It is hard to see how the editor is
organised, and docs/rules/code-quality.md says to split by concern.

GOAL
A reviewer opening the editor folder should see the structure at a
glance: which files are pure editing logic, which are export, storage
and UI. Success is a clear folder structure with behaviour unchanged:
no logic edits, every test passing, and no broken imports.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- This is a move-only change. No renaming of functions, no logic
  changes, no refactors, no new features.
- Tests move next to the code they test, if that is the repo's pattern.
  Follow the repo's existing conventions.
- Before moving anything, propose the grouping to me as a table: each
  proposed folder, its purpose, and which files go in it. Ask me to
  confirm, with your recommendation. Don't move files until I confirm.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Read all 115 files and propose the grouping, including a list of any
   file whose home is unclear and a recommendation for each.
2. After I confirm, move the files and fix every import, including
   dynamic imports, test imports and path aliases. Use git mv so
   history is kept.
3. Update ARCHITECTURE.md's module map and any README path that
   changed, so the docs stay true.

OUT OF SCOPE
Splitting video-editor.tsx or planMake, any logic change, CI files,
new dependencies.

DONE MEANS
- A clear folder structure, with no leftover flat pile.
- pnpm lint, types, formatting, check:standards, all unit tests and the
  build pass, with the same test count as before the move.
- Every e2e test passes.
- git diff shows moves with only import-path changes (show the rename
  detection summary: files renamed, files with content changes, and what
  those changes are).
- ARCHITECTURE.md's module map matches the new paths.
- CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the test counts before and after, the rename summary, and a search
proving no import still points to an old path. Say what you didn't
check.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns. Move only.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green. Don't change a test's expectations.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=26 session=b42658ff-19b2-4aa3-84fd-8e0832d6120c]
timestamp: 2026-10-11T06:37:36.336Z
time: 2026-10-11 06:37

Here's my proposed grouping for all 115 files. Nothing has moved yet.

**Conventions I'm following** (all checked in the repo):
- **Tests** already sit next to their code everywhere, so each test moves with its file.
- **Import style:** inside the editor, imports are relative (`./x`). Outside it, some use `@/app/[locale]/editor/…` and some relative (`make-plan.ts`). I'll keep each import's existing style and change only the path.
- **`actions.ts`** stays at the root, as `make/actions.ts` and `contact/actions.ts` do.
- **Plain subfolders:** Next.js only routes a folder with a `page.tsx`, and `src/**/*.test.mts` already finds nested tests. No underscore prefix and no config change is needed.

**The proposal: logic folders by concern, plus `ui/` split by area of the screen**

| Folder | Purpose | Files (each with its `.test.mts` where one exists) |
|---|---|---|
| `editor/` (root, 4) | The route and the component that wires everything | `page.tsx`, `layout.tsx`, `video-editor.tsx`, `actions.ts` |
| `model/` (25) | The edit itself: clips, cuts, timing, look, undo. Plain TS, no React components | `sequence`, `transitions`, `clip-audio`, `clip-selection`, `cleanup`, `color-grade`, `transform`, `audio-lane`, `sources`, `timeline-scale`, `preview-speed`, `edit-snapshot`, `history`, `clip-speed.test` |
| `overlays/` (12) | What's drawn on the picture: titles, captions, logos, annotations, motion, designs | `overlays`, `annotations`, `captions`, `logo`, `motion`, `text-style`, `design` |
| `audio/` (15) | Reading the recording: transcript, silences and audio problems, filler cuts, re-recording | `transcript`, `take-audio`, `audio-gaps`, `audio-quality`, `filler-words`, `filler-cuts`, `sentence-splice`, `re-record`, `use-take-analysis` |
| `assistant/` (5) | Typed or spoken commands and highlights: schemas and plans | `edit-command`, `highlights`, `voice-command` |
| `export/` (3) | Turning the edit into a file | `export-video`, `export-button` |
| `projects/` (3) | Saving and opening projects | `projects`, `editor-start` |
| `ui/shell/` (10) | The window: top bar, tool rail, save state, leaving | `editor-shell`, `tool-rail`, `editor-status`, `project-title`, `history-controls`, `leave-guard`, `studio`, `upload-dropzone`, `video-file-input`, `editor-layout-store` |
| `ui/preview/` (11) | The player and what you drag on it | `picture-frame`, `overlay-preview`, `annotation-layer`, `design-canvas`, `logo-box`, `title-box`, `zoom-box`, `item-toolbar`, `compare-toggle`, `speed-toggle`, `use-sequence-playback` |
| `ui/timeline/` (6) | The timeline and its waveform editors | `editor-timeline`, `timeline-flag`, `trim-handle`, `loudness-track`, `filler-cut-editor`, `join-editor` |
| `ui/panels/` (17) | The tool panels and their shared fields | `annotation-panel`, `audio-lane-panel`, `clip-audio-panel`, `color-panel`, `graphics-panel`, `motion-panels`, `on-video-panels`, `transform-panel`, `transition-panel`, `re-record-panel`, `transcript-panel`, `cleanup-bar`, `highlights-dialog`, `project-panel`, `more-info`, `degrees-field`, `text-style-fields` |
| `ui/assistant/` (4) | The chat | `command-bar`, `command-reply`, `voice-command-button`, `make-follow-ups` |

That's 115 files. Outside the editor, 11 files under `(app)/`, `paid-actions.test.mts`, 9 e2e files and `next.config.ts` also need their import paths updated.

**Files whose home is unclear, with my recommendation:**

| File | Recommend | Alternative | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `actions.ts` | root | `assistant/` | The repo keeps a route's server actions at its root |
| `sources.ts` | `model/` | `projects/` | Added videos are part of the edit (clips point at them). `probeVideo` uses a `<video>` element, so it isn't fully pure |
| `use-take-analysis.ts` | `audio/` | `assistant/` | It produces the transcript and audio flags. It calls Deepgram through `actions.ts` |
| `use-sequence-playback.ts` | `ui/preview/` | `model/` | A React hook that drives the media elements, not pure logic |
| `export-button.tsx` | `export/` | `ui/` | It holds the `useVideoExport` hook, keeping the whole export path in one folder |
| `editor-start.ts` | `projects/` | root | It's how a project opened from Home or Make it reaches the editor (in memory, not stored) |
| `editor-layout-store.ts` | `ui/shell/` | `projects/` | It's a layout preference saved in localStorage, not project data |
| `preview-speed.ts` | `model/` | `ui/preview/` | One constant, also used by `edit-command` |
| `clip-speed.test.mts` | `model/` | — | It tests clip speed across modules, and there's no `clip-speed.ts` |
| `make-follow-ups.tsx` | `ui/assistant/` | `assistant/` | Mostly chat UI, though it also holds `planFollowUp` |
| `cleanup-bar.tsx`, `highlights-dialog.tsx`, `project-panel.tsx` | `ui/panels/` | — | Each is a tool-rail panel (`CleanupPanel`, `HighlightsPanel`, `ProjectPanel`) |

One naming note: `editor/ui/` sits beside the app-wide `@/components/ui`. They're separate folders, but I can call it `components/` instead if you'd rather avoid the echo.

Do you confirm this grouping, including my recommendation for each unclear file? I recommend it as shown. Or tell me which rows to change.

CONTEXT
Main includes #37. An architecture review found planMake in
make-plan.ts is one function of about 510 lines doing many jobs:
reading the request, preparing input, calling Gemini, validating the
reply, the second check, deciding treatment, shape and fit, labelling
the plan card, and building the plan. docs/rules/code-quality.md says to
split by concern.

GOAL
A reviewer should be able to read planMake in a minute and find each
decision in its own small, tested function. Success is the same
behaviour with a clear structure: planMake becomes a short function that
calls named steps, and the pure steps are unit-tested on their own.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- No behaviour change. Same inputs give the same plan. Nothing I decided
  earlier changes: Gemini's treatment is only trusted when my own words
  ask, the plan card is honest about "From your brief", and screen
  recordings are never zoomed or cut out.
- Lock the behaviour first with tests, then move code.
- Before changing any code, propose the split to me as a table: each
  step, its purpose, its inputs and outputs, and whether it is pure.
  Ask me to confirm, with your recommendation. Don't change code until I
  confirm.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Read planMake and propose the split. Include anything you find that
   doesn't fit any step, with a recommendation.
2. After I confirm, first add tests that capture today's behaviour,
   replaying the saved Gemini replies (scratchpad\realtest2\raw.txt and
   the earlier runs, or equivalents in the tests) with the fake AI. They
   must pass on the unchanged code.
3. Then extract one step at a time, running the tests after each, with
   no logic changes. Pure steps get their own unit tests.
4. Update ARCHITECTURE.md's module map and PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

OUT OF SCOPE
Behaviour changes, new features, splitting video-editor.tsx, CI files,
new dependencies.

DONE MEANS
- planMake is short and calls named steps, each in a clear place.
- The behaviour-capture tests pass on the old code and the new code,
  with the same results.
- All existing unit and e2e tests pass, with the same counts plus the
  new tests.
- Tests that fail if a step's logic is changed.
- CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the before and after sizes of planMake, the test counts, and a
replay of my real runs (whole screen, promo as designed) giving
identical plans before and after. Say what you didn't check.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green. Don't change a test's expectations.
- Run formatters only on tracked files, and never touch design/.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- design/ is not committed.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

I've read all of `planMake` and the helpers it uses. Here's the proposal; no code has changed.

**First, a finding:** `planMake` (lines 928 to 1437, 510 lines) is entirely pure. Reading the request, preparing the input, calling Gemini, validating the reply and the second check aren't in it. They already live in their own functions: `planMakeIt` and `checkMakePlan` in `actions.ts`, then `checkFound` and `applyCheck`, with `make-it.tsx` chaining them. So the split covers what `planMake` actually does: cutting, fitting the length, treatment, shape, zooms, music, on-screen text, captions and the summary.

**Proposed steps** (all pure; the same inputs always give the same output):

| # | Step | Purpose | Inputs → output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cutEdit` | Today's `cutWith` closure: kept ranges, the opening moment moved first, clips, filler cuts that still apply, segments, edited length | reply's cut fields, sentences, duration, filler cuts, removeSentences, cap, opener → `{ kept, clips, cuts, joins, transitions, segments, editedSeconds }` |
| 1a | `transitionJoins` | Inside `cutEdit`: picks the ≤3 joins with the biggest gaps and sets fade/dissolve/slide on them | clips, transition → join indexes (sets `clip.transition`) |
| 2 | `lengthLimits` | Short-form or not, the asked length, the limit, the fill target | reply, request → `{ shortForm, asked, limitSeconds, target }` |
| 3 | `openingMoment` | The strongest moment to open a short-form edit | moments, sentence count, shortForm → `{ from, to }` or null |
| 4 | `fitToLimit` | Over the limit: choose whole moments to fit, then cap the source | cut inputs, limits, opener → `{ removeSentences, cut, trimmedToFit }` |
| 5 | `lengthenForCheck` | The check said too short: take back left-out sentences, never the check's own drops | same, plus `tooShort` → `{ removeSentences, cut, lengthened }` |
| 6 | `shortLabel` | `'short_form'` / `'asked'` / null, rounded as the plan card shows it | asked, limit, edited length, duration, tooShort → label |
| 7 | `screenTreatment` | Whole or designed for a screen recording, and why. Only your words ask, then a promo's wording, else whole; unchecked is always whole | subject, your words, unchecked → `{ treatment, why }` or null |
| 8 | `shapeAndFit` | The shape, and how the video goes into it: kept, matches, designed, face, centred or whole. A screen recording is never zoomed | reply, picture, shortForm, treatment, your words, pictureMatters, has words, frame times → `{ shape, fit, how, asked, design, transform }` |
| 9 | `planZooms` | Zoom cuts at the joins, then zooms on the named sentences. None on a screen recording or a designed frame | reply, joins, timings, sentences, design, screen, how → `Zoom[]` |
| 10 | `musicBed` | The track repeated under the edit, ducking | music, edited length → audio items |
| 11 | `planTitle` | A title only from words said or written; anything invented is dropped | reply, known words, length, design, tall → `{ title, titleDropped }` |
| 12 | `keyLineTitles` | Quoted key lines over their sentences, after the title, never overlapping | reply, sentences, timings, length, title → `TitleCard[]` |
| 13 | `withCallToAction` | The brief's call to action over the last seconds, replacing any key line it meets | key lines, reply, known words, length, title → `{ autoTitles, callToAction }` |
| 14 | `captionSettings` | Caption look → the edit's caption fields | captions, has words, style → caption fields |
| 15 | `planSummary` | The plan card's facts, including "From your brief" (`said` only from your own words) | everything above → `MakeSummary` |

`planMake` then becomes about 40 lines: it computes `said` (request plus follow-ups), `promo` and `known`, and calls 1 to 15 in order.

**Things that fit no single step, and my recommendation:**
- `said`, `promo` and `known` are each used by two or more steps. I'd compute them once in `planMake` and pass them in, rather than recompute them per step.
- The screen `why` is built inside the summary today. I'd move it into step 7 so the treatment and its reason are decided in one place. The output is the same.

**How I'll lock behaviour before touching code:**
- **Committed tests:** each real reply (runs 1 to 3, plus the run 2 and run 3 checks applied the way `make-it.tsx` does), plus variants covering every branch (reel with a face, a designed meeting ad, an asked length, too short, unchecked, music, follow-ups), over the test file's 79-sentence stand-in transcript (`longTalk`). Each full `edit` and `summary` is compared with a fixture written once from today's code and never regenerated.
- **Local replay with your real transcript:** your real 79-sentence transcript is kept out of the repo on purpose, so I'll replay runs 1 to 3 over it with a script that isn't committed, before and after, and diff the plans.
- **Per-step unit tests**, plus a temporary deliberate change to each step's logic to show its test fails. Those changes get reverted.

**Placement, which could reasonably go two ways:**
- **A (recommended):** the steps stay in `make-plan.ts` as exported functions just above `planMake`, grouped by section. The constants and helpers they share stay where they are, so the diff is pure extraction and easy to check for "no logic change". Splitting the 1,581-line file into several would be its own follow-up.
- **B:** a new `make/plan/` folder with one file per concern (`cut.ts`, `shape.ts`, `on-screen.ts`, `summary.ts`). That needs moving shared constants and helpers too, and risks circular imports with `make-plan.ts`.

Do you confirm this split, with placement A? I recommend it as shown. Or tell me which rows or the placement to change.

CONTEXT
Main includes #37 (and the planMake split, if merged by now: check).
Make it today takes a typed request and plans from the transcript and
pictures, asking up to 3 clarifying questions. It already understands
long briefs (tone, call to action, audience) and records on the plan
card whether each choice came from my own words ("From your brief",
"As you asked") or was chosen for me. Shapes are 9:16, 1:1, 4:5, 16:9.
Fit is Bars, Blur or Fill. Music and AI voice-over are not built yet.

GOAL
Someone with a clear vision should be able to describe it, or answer a
few quick questions, and get a plan that matches. Today my tests were
one-line requests like "make this an Instagram reel". Success is a
guided flow that asks only what the request doesn't already say, turns
the answers into the brief, and produces a plan that follows them.

DECISIONS ALREADY MADE
- Questions are fixed choices in the interface, not AI-generated. They
  cost no AI call. Code decides which to skip, based on what my request
  already says.
- Ask at most 5 questions, one screen, with "You decide" on each and a
  Skip all. Never ask what the request already answers.
- Questions, in this order:
  1. What is it: Instagram reel, TikTok, YouTube Short, ad, other.
  2. Ad only: vertical, horizontal or keep the video's shape.
  3. Reel, TikTok or Short: keep my voice, music only, or both.
  4. Music genre or mood (calm, upbeat, hip hop, cinematic, none).
  5. Tone and call to action (short optional text fields).
- A question for a feature that isn't built yet (music, voice-over) is
  defined in the list but not shown until that feature exists. Ask me
  before deciding how any answer is used if it affects an unbuilt
  feature.
- Answers I click count as my own words for the honesty rules: they may
  decide treatment, shape, fit and "From your brief". Anything I skip is
  chosen for me and labelled that way.
- A long typed brief still works on its own, and its details skip the
  matching questions.
- Keep the layout of design/app.html. Don't touch design/.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Propose the question list and the skip rules as a table (question,
   when it is asked, when it is skipped, what it changes in the plan).
   Ask me to confirm, with your recommendation. Don't build until I
   confirm.
2. After I confirm, build the question screen and the logic that turns
   answers into the brief, reusing the existing brief and plan-card
   code.
3. Make the plan follow the answers: shape and fit for ads, shape
   and fit for reels, caption and tone, and the plan card reasons.
4. Add tests with the fake AI only.

OUT OF SCOPE
Music, voice-over, style presets, transitions and animation quality,
CI files, new dependencies.

DONE MEANS
- A one-line request asks only the questions it doesn't answer; a long
  brief that answers everything asks none.
- An ad asks vertical or horizontal; a reel does not.
- "You decide" and Skip all work and are labelled as chosen for me.
- Plan card labels are honest: clicked answers read as mine, skipped
  ones as chosen.
- Unit tests for the skip rules and the answers-to-brief step, and an
  end-to-end test of the flow. Tests that fail when each rule is
  removed.
- Screenshots of the question screen on desktop and a narrow phone
  width saved to my Desktop in a folder called questions1.
- All existing tests and CI pass, with no check loosened. CI green.

HOW TO VERIFY
Replay three requests with the fake AI: a one-line reel request, a one-
line ad request, and a long brief with genre, tone, audience, call to
action and length. Show the questions asked and the plan card for each.
Say what you didn't check. I will approve one real Gemini run at the
end, but don't make it without asking.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, or a choice could
  reasonably go two ways, ask me BEFORE building. One clear question at
  a time, with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Before you start, tell me in 2-3 sentences what you understood and
  your plan. At each decision, say what you chose and why.
- Code: clean, minimal, matching existing patterns.
- Tests: fake AI only, no real API calls. Never skip, delete or loosen a
  test to get CI green.
- Run formatters only on tracked files. Never touch design/.
- Never guess on destructive or irreversible actions. Ask first.
- Git: one commit with the session log in it. Merge commits only, no
  rebase, no force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never
  hand-edit it, and check the exchange count doesn't drop. In
  PROGRESS_NOTES.md, keep both sides on conflicts.
- PR: clear title. The description has the goal, what changed, what's
  tested, what isn't, and how to try it.
- CI: wait for every check. Fix real failures yourself. A flaky test:
  rerun once. A Docker Hub rate limit on the postgres pull is not a code
  problem: rerun later.
- Update PROGRESS_NOTES.md.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences: what changed, what's tested and what isn't,
the CI result, the PR link, and anything I need to decide.

Here's the cost picture for item 2, from 47 real Make it calls in your local `ai_usage`:

| Call | Avg input tokens | Avg cost |
|---|---|---|
| Plan (`make_it`) | 54,468 | $0.0191 |
| Check (`make_it_check`) | 639 | $0.00056 |

Solving those two together gives about $0.30 per million input tokens and $2.50 per million output tokens.

**Proposal: raise the request limit from 300 to 1,000 characters.** That's roughly 150 to 180 words, enough for a brief with platform, shape, genre, tone, audience, call to action and length, with room to spare.

- **Cost:** 700 more characters is about 175 more tokens, roughly $0.00005 per call. That's about +0.3% on a plan call and about +9% on a check call (which stays well under a tenth of a cent). Worst case per Make it, with plan and check, is about $0.0001 more.
- **Where it applies:**
  - the request box;
  - `planMakeIt`'s and `checkMakePlan`'s `request` input;
  - `followUpMakeIt`'s `request`, which carries the original request back for chat follow-ups, so it has to accept the longer one too.

  The 20 earlier follow-ups and each new one keep their 300-character limit under a separate constant, so a follow-up call can't grow by more than the one longer request.
- **Server limits:** the action body limit is about 13.8 MB, dominated by the pictures, so 1,000 characters makes no difference there. The two new short answer fields (tone, call to action) would use the existing 120-character brief-part limit.

Shall I raise the request limit to 1,000 characters, with follow-ups staying at 300? I recommend 1,000. 600 would be tight for a full brief; 2,000 adds cost for no real gain.

The Projects relink test passes on its rerun, so that one is a timing flake. The other failure is a real conflict between an existing test and the behaviour you asked for, so I need your decision. I haven't changed the test.

**The test:** `make-it.spec.ts:1351`, "a brief part the user never gave is shown as chosen for them, not as their brief". Its second half sends "make it energetic" with Gemini's tone set to "energetic", then asserts the card contains no "Chosen for" at all. The test was written when "Chosen for" could only mean a brief part like the tone.

**What happens now:** "make it energetic" doesn't say what the video is, so the question screen asks "What is it?". The helper presses Skip all, and the card, as you specified, says:

> From your brief: energetic tone
> Chosen for you: an Instagram Reel

The tone is still correctly yours. The new line is the honest label for the question you skipped, and it's what breaks the blanket `not.toContainText('Chosen for')`.

Options:
1. **Recommended: make the assertion say what it means.** Replace the blanket check with two exact ones: "From your brief: energetic tone" is present (already asserted), and no "Chosen for" line contains "energetic tone". Then add an assertion that the skipped question reads "Chosen for you: an Instagram Reel". The test checks the same rule (your tone is never labelled as chosen) more precisely, and also covers the new label.
2. **Leave this test's assertions untouched and change its input** to "make this an Instagram reel, energetic". Then nothing is asked: the request names a reel, a tone and, for a reel, no shape. Only the call-to-action question would remain, so I'd have to add an ending phrase too. That changes what the test feeds in rather than what it checks.

Which do you want? I recommend option 1. It keeps the test strict about the tone and adds a check for the new label instead of removing one.
