The database is reachable now. The container was cleanly restarted at 11:31:52 UTC, and a read-only `select 1` just succeeded. I didn't start, stop, restart, delete or reset anything.

**1. How the repo starts local Supabase**
- The tool is the Supabase CLI, run through `pnpm dlx`. That downloads and runs it on the spot, so nothing is installed. It isn't a dependency in `package.json`, which is why `supabase` and `pnpm exec supabase` aren't found.
- From the repo root (README "Run it locally", and `.claude/skills/dev-onboarding/SKILL.md`):
  ```
  pnpm dlx supabase start     # Postgres and Auth in Docker (first run downloads ~2 GB)
  pnpm dlx supabase status    # prints the local URLs and keys
  pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy
  ```
- It needs Docker Desktop running (on Windows, with WSL2).
- `supabase/config.toml` sets `project_id = "t1-alishbasaboor"`, the API on 54321 and the database on 54322. Nothing under `docs/` covers starting Supabase.

**2. `docker ps -a`**

| Container | Status | Ports |
|---|---|---|
| `supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 6 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54322->5432/tcp`, `[::]:54322->5432/tcp` |
| `supabase_kong_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54321->8000/tcp` |
| `supabase_auth_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `9999/tcp` (not published) |
| `supabase_inbucket_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54324->8025/tcp` |

- **The database container is running, not exited.** Its restart count is 0 and its restart policy is `unless-stopped`.
- **Its log shows a clean stop at 11:31:50 and a start two seconds later**, so it was stopped and started rather than crashing. On start it printed "Database directory appears to contain a database; Skipping initialization", so the data was kept. It has accepted connections since 11:31:52.
- **That restart explains my failed attempts.** The other containers were up while I was getting connection refusals.

**3. If it stops answering again, run these in order, from the repo root**
1. Make sure Docker Desktop is open and shows "Engine running".
2. `docker ps -a`, and look at `supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor`.
3. If it shows `Exited`: `docker start supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor`. This starts the existing container with its existing volume, so no data is lost.
4. If several containers are down, or step 3 isn't enough: `pnpm dlx supabase start`. This starts the existing containers and volumes and doesn't reinitialise a database that already has data, so no data is lost.
5. `pnpm dlx supabase status` should list the DB URL on port 54322.
6. If the container is up and healthy but connections are still refused, the port forwarding is stuck. Quit and reopen Docker Desktop (tray icon → Quit Docker Desktop, then start it again), then repeat steps 2–5. This doesn't touch volumes, so no data is lost.

Never use `supabase db reset`, `supabase stop --no-backup`, `docker compose down -v`, `docker volume prune` or `docker rm -v`. Those can delete the data.

Since it answers now, I can run `pnpm test:db` locally whenever you say. Nothing is committed yet.

Retake is my local-first AI video editor. It runs on my computer only
and will not be deployed. Its three main features are:
1. Make it with AI: turns a recording into a reel, ad or TikTok. The

2. Manual editing: a timeline editor with trim, split, merge, reorder,
   captions, titles, logo, zoom, colour, speed, transitions, an audio
   lane, before/after comparison and export.
3. Automatic cleanup and re-recording: transcript, silence and filler
   word detection, one-click cleanup, per-filler cuts, and re-recording
   one sentence and splicing it back.
Stack: Next.js 16, React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind 4, next-intl, Prisma
with Postgres, local Supabase (Auth) in Docker, Gemini and Deepgram for
AI, browser-side export, IndexedDB for projects, pnpm, GitHub Actions.
The README is out of date: it has changed several times, and the
contact form and its CONTACT_IP_SECRET setting were removed. Main is the
current state.

GOAL
A short, simple, true README that someone can read in two minutes and
use to run the project. Success is that every command in it works from
a clean clone and every path exists, and nothing in it overclaims.

WHAT TO DO

to me, and wait for my yes before touching the repo file.

2. The three main features, a short list each, in plain words.

RULES FOR THE CONTENT

RULES

The README draft is in the scratchpad (`README.draft.md`). The repo file is untouched, and no branch exists yet. Here it is in full:

````markdown
# Retake

An AI video editor for fixing and polishing recordings you already have.
Load a video, and Retake helps you clean it up, edit it by hand, or turn it
into something new, without shooting it again.

## What it does

**Make it with AI.** Turns a recording into a reel, an ad or a TikTok.

- Type what you want, answer a few quick questions, and approve the plan before anything changes.
- Pick one of four looks: Clean, Bold, Cinematic or Playful.

**Manual editing.** A timeline editor.

- Trim, split, merge and reorder clips.
- Add captions, titles, a logo, zoom, colour, speed changes and transitions.
- An audio lane, a before/after comparison, and export to a video file.

**Automatic cleanup and re-recording.**

- A transcript, with silences and filler words found for you.
- One-click cleanup, or cut fillers one at a time.
- Re-record a single sentence and splice it back in.

## How it works

Your video and your projects stay in the browser: editing, preview and export
run there, and saved projects are kept in the browser's storage (IndexedDB).
For the AI features only, a video's audio goes to Deepgram (transcripts and
spoken commands), and Gemini receives text (typed commands and the
transcript) and, for Make it, small still frames taken every 2 seconds. The
video file itself is never uploaded. Sign-in runs on a local Supabase in
Docker. The server also downloads AI model prices from OpenRouter, which
receives none of your data. Sentry and PostHog send nothing unless their keys
are set. Details are on the Privacy page, at `/privacy`
([source](<src/app/[locale]/(site)/privacy/page.tsx>)).

## Stack

- Next.js 16 and React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS 4, next-intl
- Postgres through Prisma
- Supabase Auth, run locally in Docker
- Gemini and Deepgram for the AI features
- Browser-side export, with projects saved in IndexedDB
- pnpm, Playwright, GitHub Actions

## Folder structure

```
.github/        CI workflows
docs/           engineering rules and CI/CD setup
e2e/            Playwright end-to-end tests
messages/       UI text (en.json)
prisma/         database schema and migrations
public/         static files, including Make it's music
scripts/        repo checks and database helpers
supabase/       local Supabase config
src/
  app/          pages: landing, privacy, sign-in, home, projects, Make it, the editor
  components/   shared UI components
  i18n/         locale routing
  lib/          shared code: env, errors, analytics, flags, Supabase helpers
  server/       server actions, AI provider wrappers, database client
```

## Run it locally

You need:

- Node 22.18 or later, and pnpm
- Docker Desktop (for local Supabase)
- Optional: a Gemini API key and a Deepgram API key

Then, from the repo root:

```bash
pnpm install                      # also generates the Prisma client
pnpm dlx supabase start           # Postgres and Auth in Docker
pnpm dlx supabase status          # prints the local URLs and keys
cp .env.example .env.local        # then fill it in (below)
pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy   # create the tables
pnpm dev                          # http://localhost:3000
```

In `.env.local`, set these from `pnpm dlx supabase status`: `DATABASE_URL`
and `DIRECT_URL` (the local Postgres), `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`. Create an account on the sign-in page
to use the app.

The AI keys go in `.env.local` too:

- `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY`: transcripts and spoken commands. Cleanup,
  re-recording, highlights and Make it all start from the transcript.
- `GEMINI_API_KEY`: typed commands, highlights and Make it's plan.

Without them the app still runs, and the editor and manual editing work;
only the AI features fail, with an error message.

## Tests

```bash
pnpm test        # unit tests (node --test)
pnpm test:e2e    # Playwright end-to-end tests; starts the app unless E2E_BASE_URL is set
```

No test calls a real AI provider.

## Architecture

Decisions, trade-offs, scope and known limits are in
[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md).

## Scope

Retake is local-first: it runs on your own computer and is not deployed.
````

Where the draft differs from your brief, and why:
- **No database-tests line.** `pnpm test:db` isn't on main; it's only in the unmerged PR 3 branch.
- **No quoted error message.** The old README said a missing AI key shows "The AI isn't available right now." In main's code, that message is only for a key the provider *refuses*. An unset key fails as a generic error, so the draft says only "fail, with an error message".
- **Both keys for Make it and highlights.** Both read the Deepgram transcript, so they need both keys, and the key lines say so.
- **Every command and path in the draft comes from main's `package.json`, `git ls-tree` and the source.** I'll run the read-only command checks and list how I checked each one once you approve. Running them now would load the machine while the PR 3 e2e suite is still going on :3200.
- **The branch comes after PR 3.** My working tree still holds PR 3's uncommitted work, so I'll create the README branch from main only after PR 3 is committed and pushed.

**One question:** your brief says audio goes to Deepgram and still frames to Gemini, and "nothing else leaves the computer". Main's code and Privacy page show three more things:
- Gemini also gets text: typed commands and the transcript.
- The server downloads AI prices from OpenRouter, with none of your data.
- Sentry and PostHog send data if their keys are set.

**I recommend keeping the draft's fuller paragraph, which states all three**, because "nothing else" wouldn't be true. Is that wording OK, or do you want it shorter?

# Retake: architecture and session-log scores

Two independent scores for main at `6680bcb`: architecture 10 of 15 and session logs 10 of 15. I read the code and the logs directly, then checked the docs against the code. I changed nothing, ran nothing that writes, and printed no keys or personal data.

## 1. Architecture: 10 / 15

I judged it as a local-first app.

| Area | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Server boundary and the AI path | 2.5 / 3 | See below |
| Data and persistence | 2.5 / 3 | See below |
| Client structure | 1.5 / 3 | See below |
| Test architecture | 2 / 3 | See below |
| Docs accuracy and repo hygiene | 1.5 / 3 | See below |

**Server boundary and the AI path (2.5).**
- Every paid action goes through one wrapper, `validatedAction`. In order, it checks the session (failing closed with a 401), validates the input with Zod, then takes from the daily allowance.
- Taking from the allowance is one conditional update. If two first calls of the day race, the database's unique key catches it (Prisma's P2002 error) and the request is counted again.
- Each AI provider is called from one file. ESLint bans the Gemini SDK and Deepgram's host anywhere else.
- Each Gemini feature has an output-token cap that is checked when the code compiles.
- Request sizes are limited on the server, not just in the browser.
- **Lost half a mark** for how an unset AI key is handled, and because one time limit is optional (items 5 and 6 below).

**Data and persistence (2.5).**
- Prisma only, CI writes the migrations, and every table has row-level security with no policies.
- An IndexedDB save is reported done only when its transaction commits.
- `ai_usage` has a fixed column contract with the MOAD dashboard.
- **Lost half a mark** because spend can't be traced to an account.

**Client structure (1.5).**
- `model/` is mostly plain TypeScript; only `color-grade.ts` and `sources.ts` touch the browser.
- But the editor's state lives in one huge component, and `make-plan.ts` is 2,058 lines.

**Test architecture (2).**
- 716 unit tests, 142 e2e tests, and now real-Postgres tests.
- But e2e swaps in canned results for every server action, CI runs with accounts off, and only one paid action is tested through its real path.

**Docs accuracy and repo hygiene (1.5).**
- ARCHITECTURE.md is unusually candid.
- But it has several stale or wrong lines, the PROGRESS_NOTES.md summary is stale, and about 1,340 lines of template code are unused.

### What still costs marks, most expensive first

1. **The editor component is too big.** (Size: large.)
   - **Where:** `src/app/[locale]/editor/video-editor.tsx:236–2807`.
   - **Why:** one function of about 2,570 lines, holding 53 `useState`, 15 `useRef` and 9 `useEffect`, with no `useMemo` or `useCallback`. Every change touches it, and the React Compiler is the only thing keeping re-renders in check.
   - **Smallest fix:** move state into hooks by concern (playback, the edit history, the assistant, projects), one per PR.
2. **The Make it planner is one big file.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `src/app/[locale]/(app)/make/make-plan.ts`: 2,058 lines and 88 exports.
   - **Why:** the reply's shape, the planner's steps and the plan comparison are all in one file. `planMake` itself is fine at 82 lines (1828–1909).
   - **Smallest fix:** split into a reply-shape file, a steps file and a summary file, with re-exports so callers don't change.
3. **Unused template code.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `src/lib/rbac` plus `src/lib/flags` (1,339 lines with their tests), `src/server/actions/feature-flags.ts`, `src/server/api/route-factory.ts`, the `/admin` route, and `.github/workflows/deploy-migrations.yml`, which targets a production database that doesn't exist.
   - **Why:** dead code that a reviewer has to read and that has to be maintained.
   - **Smallest fix:** delete it in one PR. `validatedAction`'s `authorize` option and `provision-user.ts` import from it, so trim those too.
4. **Docs that contradict the code.** (Size: small.)
   - `ARCHITECTURE.md:270–273` says an unset AI key shows "The AI isn't available right now." **The code shows otherwise:** `google.ts:31` and `deepgram.ts:69` throw a plain `Error` with no status, so `aiFailureOf` returns null and the error goes out as a generic bug report.
   - `ARCHITECTURE.md:245–246` gives the component's lines as 236–2793; it now ends at 2807.
   - "What I'd do next" item 2 (`:294–296`) is done (#51), and "Known limits" doesn't mention the `db-tests` job.
   - PROGRESS_NOTES.md's "Read this first" box still says:
     - planMake is "63 lines" (it's 82);
     - "Make it steps on Pricing" (the Pricing page is gone);
     - the contact limit is in place and "Production needs `CONTACT_IP_SECRET`" (both removed in #50).
   - **Smallest fix:** edit those lines.
5. **An unset AI key is reported as a bug, not a setup problem.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `src/server/ai/google.ts:31`, `src/server/ai/deepgram.ts:69`.
   - **Why:** the user sees the generic error, Sentry gets a false bug report, and the log never names the setting to check.
   - **Smallest fix:** throw an error that `aiFailureOf` classifies as `'key'`.
6. **Gemini's time limit is optional.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `src/server/ai/google.ts:82` (`timeoutMs?: number`).
   - **Why:** every current caller passes one, but nothing enforces it; Deepgram's is already required.
   - **Smallest fix:** make it required.
7. **Two round trips to Supabase on every paid action.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `src/proxy.ts:25–60`, then `requireUser` at `session.ts:22`.
   - **Why:** each action checks the session twice, and that's where the slow requests behind today's e2e flakes were (one `proxy.ts` took 4.0 s).
   - **Smallest fix:** let a POST that carries a `next-action` header skip the proxy's `getUser`, since the action checks the session itself.
8. **e2e never runs a server action's real handler.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `e2e/editor-helpers.ts:160–195` empties the input and swaps in a canned result.
   - **Why:** this is clever, but six of the seven paid actions are only unit-tested with mocks.
   - **Smallest fix:** extend `paid-action.db-test.mts` to the transcript, highlights and Make it.
9. **The allowance is given back for any handler error.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `src/server/actions/validated-action.ts:56–63`.
   - **Why:** a provider can answer (and charge for it) and then a bug in parsing the reply gives the allowance back anyway.
   - **Smallest fix:** give it back only for errors from the provider call.

### Fine, or deliberate trade-offs
- The single place for AI calls, the cost tracking and ESLint enforcing both.
- Unknown prices are stored as null, never zero.
- Sign-in fails closed even though the proxy fails open.
- Export by recording in the browser, and projects in IndexedDB.
- 5-minute caps enforced on the server.
- Make it's hidden music question: `public/music/` holds only `LICENSES.md`, so `loadTrack` returns null. This matches "planned".

### What wouldn't hold up if deployed
- **Request size:** the body limit is about 13.8 MB, against Vercel's 4.5 MB.
- **Duration:** actions run up to 60 s, with no `maxDuration` set.
- **Spend:** there's no ceiling across all accounts, and `ai_usage` has no user id.
- **Sign-up:** no email confirmation or captcha.
- **Sign-in rate limits:** sign-in runs as a server action, so Supabase's per-IP limits see the server's address.
- **Projects** live in one browser.
- **Export** runs in real time with the tab in front.
- **Migrations** are applied separately from any app deploy.

ARCHITECTURE.md's Scope table states most of these honestly.

**A stricter reviewer** would give about 8.5, for the component, the planner, the dead code and the docs drift. **A kinder one** would give about 12, for the AI boundary, the honesty of the docs and the test volume.

## 2. Claude logs: 10 / 15

I read all 8 logs, about 364 logged prompts in all.

| Area | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Goal and plan for each task | 2 / 3 | See below |
| Decisions with reasons | 1.5 / 2 | See below |
| Tests, PR and CI for each task | 2 / 3 | See below |
| You directing the work | 3.5 / 4 | See below |
| Completeness and readability | 1 / 3 | See below |

**Goal and plan (2).**
- The later prompts have GOAL, DONE MEANS, HOW TO VERIFY and RULES: 29 prompts in the latest log have explicit goal headings, and 34 ask for a plan or approval first.
- The first sessions are loose. For example, the second session's opening task is "start building a small, real, visible piece".

**Decisions with reasons (1.5).**
- Claude's replies usually give the trade-off, but your own reasons for choices are rarely written down.

**Tests, PR and CI (2).**
- The first four logs contain no PR links, and the first session pushed straight to `origin/main`.
- From the 10-07 log on, the replies carry PR links (28 and 23), CI results and test counts.

**You directing the work (3.5).** The logs show real direction:
- guardrails: fake AI only, never loosen a test, no force push;
- precise catches, such as P88, which lists wrong line numbers and wrong claims;
- sensible flake handling ("rerun only the failed jobs… don't edit the CI workflow");
- repeated demands for "say what you didn't check".

**Completeness and readability (1).**
- About 99 of the 364 "prompts" (27%) are system task notifications logged as if you'd typed them.
- 13 prompts have no reply recorded: 3 in the 10-01 log, 1 in 10-05, 3 in 10-07 and 6 in 10-10.
- The 10-01 log has a duplicated prompt (P6 and P7).
- The 10-02 log has replies recorded several times over: 11 replies to 7 prompts.
- The 10-04 log is a one-prompt hand-off with no work in it.
- The logs hold only prompts and final replies, so a test result or a CI status is a claim with no evidence beside it.
- Interim replies get overwritten when a log is regenerated.
- The long RULES block, repeated in almost every prompt, makes the latest log about 7,600 lines.
- Every header carries your git email.

### What they show well
- A clear move from vibe-coding to specified, verified, PR-based work.
- You owning scope decisions and stopping work that goes off track.

### What they show badly
- The early work has no PR or CI trail.
- The noise and gaps above.
- Little of your own reasoning recorded.

### What would make future logs stronger
- **Open each task** with a one-line ID and goal ("Task: PR-N, …"), and ask for a closing block: PR link, CI run id and result, tests run, what wasn't checked.
- **State your reason** when you choose an option ("I chose X because…"), so the decisions are yours on the page.
- **Keep the RULES short,** or refer to them once ("standing rules as before"), and keep one task per session.
- **Fix the log script** so it leaves out `<task-notification>` entries and keeps interim replies.
- **Keep your git email out of the headers.** That's a change to the log script, so the email isn't committed in every log.

### What I couldn't judge
- Tool calls and their outputs aren't in the logs, so I couldn't verify any claim a reply makes.
- Any sessions before 2026-10-01, or logs that were never committed.
- Interim replies already lost when logs were regenerated.

A stricter reviewer would give about 8, mostly for the noise and the early direct-to-main commits; a kinder one about 12, for the quality of the later direction.

CONTEXT
An independent review of main (6680bcb) found small, cheap problems.
Verify each against the code first and tell me which are true:
1. ARCHITECTURE.md:270-273 says an unset AI key shows "The AI isn't
   available right now"; the code throws a plain Error
   (google.ts:31, deepgram.ts:69), so aiFailureOf returns null and it
   is reported as a bug. Line numbers in ARCHITECTURE.md:245-246 are
   stale, "What I'd do next" item 2 is done, and Known limits omits
   the db-tests job. PROGRESS_NOTES.md's "Read this first" box says
   planMake is 63 lines, mentions Pricing, and mentions the contact
   limit and CONTACT_IP_SECRET (both removed in #50).
2. An unset AI key should be classified as a setup problem ('key'),
   not an unknown error.
3. Gemini's timeoutMs is optional (google.ts:82); Deepgram's is
   required.
4. validated-action.ts:56-63 gives the allowance back for any handler
   error, even when the provider answered.

GOAL
Fix the cheap findings so the docs are true of the code and the
failure handling is correct.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from main.
1. Fix the docs and notes so every statement is true of main.
2. Throw an error for an unset key that aiFailureOf classifies as
   'key', so users see the generic message and the log names the
   setting to check, with no key values.
3. Make Gemini's timeoutMs required.
4. Give the allowance back only for errors from the provider call.
   Propose the exact rule first and wait for my yes, because a wrong
   rule could let a charged call count as free.

OUT OF SCOPE
Splitting files, removing scaffolding, CI files, the log script.

DONE MEANS
- Tests that fail when each fix is removed (an unset key, a missing
  timeout, a parse error after a provider reply keeps the allowance
  used, a provider error gives it back).
- Existing tests that must change are shown old and new side by side
  with the reason, and I say yes first.
- CI green on every job.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. Ask one question at a time with your
  recommendation.
- Fake AI only. Never skip, delete or loosen a test. Never touch
  design/ or the servers.
- One commit with the session log. Merge commits only, no rebase, no

Two existing tests have to change. Here they are side by side; I'll wait for your yes before touching either.

**1. `src/server/actions/paid-actions.test.mts:411–423`.** The test's title and its timeout assertion change.

- **Reason:** under the approved rule, a timeout keeps the allowance used, because we stopped waiting and the provider may still have finished and billed. The test's other assertions (Deepgram 500 given back, an unusable Gemini answer counted) are unchanged.

```ts
// OLD
test('a provider that fails or times out gives the call back; one that answers, even unusably, counts', async () => {
	...
	// Gemini never answers: given up at its limit, and given back.
	geminiAnswer = hangUntilAborted
	const { result } = await settledAfter(30_000, PAID[0]!.call)
	assert.equal((result as { status?: number }).status, 504)
	assert.equal(used('command', today), 0)

// NEW
test('a provider that refuses gives the call back; one that answers, even unusably, or times out, counts', async () => {
	...
	// Gemini never answers: given up at its limit, and kept used (it may still have run and been charged).
	geminiAnswer = hangUntilAborted
	const { result } = await settledAfter(30_000, PAID[0]!.call)
	assert.equal((result as { status?: number }).status, 504)
	assert.equal(used('command', today), 1)
```

The later assertion in the same test (`used('command', today)` after the unusable answer) then expects 2 instead of 1, because the timed-out call is now counted:

```ts
// OLD
	assert.equal(answered.ok, true)
	assert.equal(used('command', today), 1)
// NEW
	assert.equal(answered.ok, true)
	assert.equal(used('command', today), 2)
```

**2. `src/server/ai/google.test.mts:183–186`.** The call gets a time limit.

- **Reason:** `timeoutMs` becomes required, so this call no longer type-checks without one. The test checks the output cap, which is unaffected.

```ts
// OLD
	await generateContent(
		{ feature: 'parse_edit_command', model: 'gemini-3.5-flash-lite' },
		{ contents: 'x', config: { maxOutputTokens: 999_999 } }
	)

// NEW
	await generateContent(
		{ feature: 'parse_edit_command', model: 'gemini-3.5-flash-lite', timeoutMs: 30_000 },
		{ contents: 'x', config: { maxOutputTokens: 999_999 } }
	)
```

Everything else is new tests: one for each row of the rule table, plus the unset key and the missing time limit.

May I make these two changes?

**How I checked:**
- Every module path and every exported name (70 names) was searched across `src`, `e2e`, `scripts` and the configs.
- No dynamic imports or string paths name them.
- `eslint.config.mjs` and `next.config.ts` don't reference them.

**`check:standards`** requires none of the modules. It does require every file in `docs/rules/` to be linked from AGENTS.md, and that no AGENTS.md link points at a missing file.

**`check:env`** checks only one direction: every variable read in `src/` must be declared. Removing the only reader of `CRON_SECRET` leaves it declared but unread, which the check allows. I'd leave `CRON_SECRET` in `env.ts`, `.env.example` and `ci.yml`, since CI files are out of scope.

**What stays, and why:**
- **The Prisma models** `AdminMember`, `AdminRole`, `FeatureFlag` and `FeatureFlagOverride`. Removing them would write a migration dropping their tables, and this task rules out migrations. The tables stay, unused; the PR will say so.
- **The User model.** It moves from `example.prisma` to `user.prisma` unchanged, including its `adminMember` back-relation.
- **`provisionUser`.** The login actions call it to upsert the User row; only its admin-seat half goes.

**`deploy-migrations.yml`** (73 lines; I'll ask about it separately):
- On a push to main, it runs `prisma migrate deploy`, the run-once SQL and a drift check against `secrets.PRODUCTION_DATABASE_URL` in a `production-database` environment.

No reply text is recorded twice in any of the 8 transcripts, so the "duplicates" are only the interim/final pairs. That gives a full picture of what the script does.

**How it builds an entry**
1. On every prompt you submit (`UserPromptSubmit`) and every time Claude stops (`Stop`), the hook runs the script.
2. The script takes the project's most recently modified `.jsonl` transcript.
3. It keeps every `user` line whose content is a string and doesn't start with `<command-name>`, `<local-command` or `Caveat:`, and isn't `isMeta`. These become prompts.
4. It keeps every Claude text block (not thinking or tool calls).
5. `dedupe_entries` then keeps **only the last Claude text before each next "prompt"**.
6. It rewrites the whole `.md` file each time.

**Why each problem happens** (counts from this session's transcript)

| Problem | Cause |
|---|---|
| Notifications counted as prompts | Background-task results arrive as `user` lines starting `<task-notification>`, which no filter excludes: 29 here. Context summaries ("This session is being continued…", `isCompactSummary: true`) get through the same way: 4 here. Of 125 "prompts", 92 are yours. |
| Replies missing | A reply goes to whatever "prompt" came last. When a notification arrives mid-turn, Claude's final reply attaches to the notification (26 replies here), and your prompt keeps only an earlier interim text, or nothing if none came before. Prompt → summary (context ran out mid-turn) and prompt → prompt (you sent the next message first) also leave gaps. |
| "Duplicates" | No text is recorded twice in any transcript (checked on all 8). The repeated `RESPONSE num=` entries in the 10-02 log are an interim reply plus the final one, from an earlier script version, kept when you chose to keep the committed version. |
| Interim replies overwritten | `dedupe_entries` keeps one text per prompt, so 842 of the 961 Claude messages in this session are dropped. Because every hook run rewrites the whole file, an interim reply that was the latest when a log was committed disappears at the next run. |
| Email in every header | `get_git_email()` writes `git config user.email` into the `git_email:` field. |

**Proposed diff** (not applied):

```diff
--- ~/.claude/extract-log.py
+++ ~/.claude/extract-log.py (proposed)
@@ def get_github_user() -> str:
-def get_git_email() -> str:
-    """Get the git config user.email for attribution."""
-    try:
-        result = subprocess.run(
-            ['git', 'config', 'user.email'],
-            capture_output=True,
-            text=True,
-            timeout=5
-        )
-        if result.returncode == 0 and result.stdout.strip():
-            return result.stdout.strip()
-    except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, FileNotFoundError):
-        pass
-    return 'unknown'
-
-
@@ def is_user_prompt(entry: dict) -> bool:
     if entry.get('isMeta') or entry.get('isSidechain'):
         return False
+    # Context summaries written when the conversation is compacted: not typed by the user.
+    if entry.get('isCompactSummary'):
+        return False
@@
     if isinstance(content, str):
+        # Background-task results the harness injects as user lines: not typed by the user.
+        if content.lstrip().startswith('<task-notification>'):
+            return False
         if content.startswith('<command-name>'):
@@ def extract_clean_log(jsonl_path: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str, str]]:
     entries = []
+    seen = set()  # (message id, text): one entry per reply, even if a line repeats
@@
         elif entry.get('type') == 'assistant':
             text = extract_assistant_text(entry)
-            if text:
+            key = (entry.get('message', {}).get('id'), text)
+            if text and key not in seen:
+                seen.add(key)
                 entries.append((timestamp, 'assistant', text))
@@ def dedupe_entries(entries):
-    """Keep only the final assistant response before each user prompt."""
-    if not entries:
-        return []
-
-    result = []
-    pending_assistant = None
-
-    for timestamp, role, content in entries:
-        if role == 'user':
-            if pending_assistant:
-                result.append(pending_assistant)
-                pending_assistant = None
-            result.append((timestamp, role, content))
-        elif role == 'assistant':
-            pending_assistant = (timestamp, role, content)
-
-    if pending_assistant:
-        result.append(pending_assistant)
-
-    return result
+    """Every reply in order, each once: interim replies are kept, not overwritten."""
+    return list(entries)
@@ def main():
     author = get_git_author()
     github_user = get_github_user()
-    git_email = get_git_email()
     lines = ["---"]
@@
     lines.append(f"github_user: {github_user}")
-    lines.append(f"git_email: {git_email}")
     lines.append(f"project: {cwd.name}")
```

- **The entry format is unchanged.** A prompt with interim replies gets several `RESPONSE` entries with the same `num`, as in the 10-02 log.
- **Header fields are unchanged, except that `git_email` is removed.** Its value comes only from git config, so nothing else can leak it.
- **Not changed:** the script picks "the most recently modified transcript in the project", so two sessions running at once could write the wrong log. That's out of scope, but you should know.

One conflict needs your decision. `total_exchanges` counts logged "prompts". Excluding the 29 notifications and 4 summaries takes this session's count from 125 to 92, even though none of your 92 prompts and none of Claude's replies are lost.

- **Task notifications are no longer logged as prompts.** Neither are the summaries written when the context fills up (`isCompactSummary`).

the planner in a small file, with behaviour unchanged.

CONTEXT
An independent review of main ranked this as the second most expensive
finding: src/app/[locale]/(app)/make/make-plan.ts is about 2,058 lines
with 88 exports. It holds the reply's schema, the planner's steps (the
extracted steps from PR #38), the summary and the plan comparison.
planMake itself is short (about 82 lines). The 100-scenario replay
fixture, built from the old code, protects the behaviour. Main is the
current state.

GOAL
Split the file by concern so each part is readable on its own, with
no behaviour change. Success is the replay fixture passing unchanged,
no circular imports, and no caller needing a different import path.

WHAT TO DO
New branch from fresh main.
1. Read the file and propose the split as a table: each new file, its
   purpose, the exports it holds and its size, and anything that fits
   nowhere. Show the dependency direction between files so there are
   no cycles. Keep make-plan.ts as a thin file that re-exports, so
   callers don't change. Wait for my yes.
2. After my yes: pure moves, no logic changes, no renames, one concern
   at a time, running the tests after each move.
3. Update ARCHITECTURE.md's module map and PROGRESS_NOTES.md so they are
   true.

OUT OF SCOPE
Any behaviour change, new features, the editor split, CI files.

DONE MEANS
- The 100-scenario replay fixture and all existing tests pass unchanged.
  Prove it: same test counts before and after, and show git's rename
  detection (files moved vs files changed, and what the changes are).
- A search proves no circular import and no leftover import of a moved
  name from the wrong place.
- make-plan.ts is a thin re-export file, and no new file is above about
  600 lines.
- The app builds, lint, typecheck and check:standards pass without
  loosening anything. CI green on every job.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. Ask one question at a time with your
  recommendation. Don't ask about things you can check yourself.
- Fake AI only. Never skip, delete or loosen a test. A test that must
  change is shown old and new side by side with the reason, and I say
  yes first. Never touch design/ or the servers.
- One commit with the session log. Merge commits only, no rebase, no
  force push. Regenerate the log with the script, never by hand.
  Commit subject lowercase after the colon, validated directly. Check
  CI once through the API and report every job. Don't merge.

created, and what you did not check.

Here's the proposed split. Every declaration is assigned (nothing fits nowhere), the sizes are measured, and the dependency check found no cycle between the new files.

| New file | Purpose | Exports it holds | Size (declarations + imports) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `make-reply.ts` | The contract with Gemini: limits and lists, the reply's Zod and JSON schemas, the check's reply | `MAKE_STYLES`, `MakeStyle`, `MAKE_FORMATS`, `MakeFormat`, `MAKE_SHAPES`, `MakeShape`, `MAKE_FITS`, `MakeFit`, `SCREEN_TREATMENTS`, `ScreenTreatment`, `MAKE_SUBJECTS`, `FRAME_EVERY_SECONDS`, `MAX_MAKE_SECONDS`, `MAX_FRAMES`, `MAX_QUESTIONS`, `PLAN_TIMEOUT_MS`, `CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, `PAGE_GRACE_MS`, `MAX_REQUEST_LENGTH`, `MAX_FOLLOW_UP_LENGTH`, `MAX_ANSWER_LENGTH`, `FRAME_SEPARATOR`, `frameTimes`, `clipText`, `CAPTION_LOOKS`, `CaptionLook`, `MAKE_TRANSITIONS`, `makeReplySchema`, `MakeReply`, `MAKE_REPLY_JSON_SCHEMA`, `MakeAnswer`, `followUpPlan`, `checkReplySchema`, `CheckReply`, `CHECK_REPLY_JSON_SCHEMA`, `checkFound`, `applyCheck` (37) | ~305 |
| `make-request.ts` | What the request's own words ask: a promo, text only, the whole screen, a fit, short form, captions, quoted lines, invented names | `askedTreatment`, `SHORT_FORM_MAX_SECONDS`, `SHORT_FORM_TARGET_SECONDS`, `isShortForm`, `saidInBrief`, `quotedLine`, `inventedNames` (7) | ~92 |
| `make-context.ts` | The plan's shapes and the context built from the input | `MusicTrack`, `BriefPart`, `MakeSummary`, `PlannedEdit`, `MadePlan`, `isPlanEdit`, `MakeBasis`, `MakeInput`, `PlanContext`, `CutBasis`, `FACE_CORNER`, `webcamOverScreen`, `planContext`, `Opener`, `LengthLimits`, `AskedLength` (16) | ~315 |
| `make-cut.ts` | What's kept and how long it runs | `keptRanges`, `chooseSentences`, `MadeCut`, `cutEdit`, `transitionJoins`, `lengthLimits`, `askedLength`, `openingMoment`, `FittedCut`, `fitToLimit`, `lengthenForCheck`, `shortLabel` (12) | ~460 |
| `make-look.ts` | How it looks: shape and fit, screen treatment, zooms, music bed, titles, key lines, call to action, captions | `faceFollow`, `screenTreatment`, `shapeAndFit`, `planZooms`, `musicBed`, `planTitle`, `keyLineTitles`, `withCallToAction`, `captionSettings` (9) | ~475 |
| `make-summary.ts` | The plan card's facts, plan-to-plan changes, and the plan described for the check | `summaryWithLength`, `planSummary`, `PlanChange`, `FitLabel`, `planChanges`, `describePlan` (6) | ~255 |
| `make-plan.ts` (kept) | `planMake` (91 lines), plus named re-exports of the other 87 original exports | `planMake` + re-exports | ~150 |

**Dependency direction** (each file imports only from the files after its arrow):

```
make-reply    -> (none of the new files)
make-request  -> make-reply
make-context  -> make-reply, make-request
make-cut      -> make-context, make-reply, make-request
make-look     -> make-cut, make-context, make-reply, make-request
make-summary  -> make-cut, make-context, make-reply, make-request
make-plan     -> all of the above (planMake + re-exports)
```

**What isn't a pure cut-and-paste:**
- **Ten private names cross a file boundary** and need `export` added in their new file: `ASKED_FILL`, `CAPTIONS_REQUEST`, `FIT_REQUEST`, `PROMO_REQUEST`, `wordsOf` (`make-request`); `MIN_PIECE_SECONDS`, `clamp`, `overlaps`, `without` (`make-cut`); and `SHAPE_RATIO` (`make-look`).
  - Nothing is renamed, and no logic changes.
  - `make-plan.ts` re-exports only the original 88 names, by name, so its public surface stays exactly the same.
- **No caller changes.** All 19 importers, including the e2e helpers and tests, keep importing from `./make-plan`.

**The cycles that already exist on main.** `make-plan.ts` imports values from `make-questions.ts` and `make-presets.ts`, and both import types back from `./make-plan`. They're type-only, so they don't exist at runtime. After the split:
- `make-reply.ts` imports the value `MAX_BRIEF_PART_LENGTH` from `make-questions.ts`.
- So if `make-questions.ts`, `make-presets.ts` and `music-track.ts` keep importing their types from the barrel, the type cycles run through every new file.

# Independent scores for main (`d131d7b`)

**Architecture scores 10.5 / 15 and the Claude logs 10.5 / 15.** I read the code and the logs directly and checked each claim against the code. I changed nothing, ran nothing that writes, and printed no keys or personal data.

## 1. Architecture: 10.5 / 15

| Area | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Server boundary and the AI path | 2.5 / 3 | See below |
| Data and persistence | 2.5 / 3 | See below |
| Client structure | 1.5 / 3 | See below |
| Test architecture | 2 / 3 | See below |
| Docs accuracy and hygiene | 2 / 3 | See below |

**Server boundary and the AI path (2.5 / 3)**
- All 7 paid actions go through `validatedAction`, which checks sign-in (refusing when unsure), then the input, then the daily allowance.
- The limits (300 / 200 / 30 / 30 / 60), time limits (30 or 60 s) and output caps (1,024 / 8,192) all match the docs.
- The allowance comes back only when the provider certainly didn't charge.
- An unset AI key is reported as a setup problem, not a bug.
- **Loses half a mark:** every paid action makes two round trips to Supabase for the same session check.

**Data and persistence (2.5 / 3)**
- Prisma is the only database access; tables have row-level security with no policies; CI writes the migrations.
- IndexedDB saves count as done only when the transaction commits.
- **Loses half a mark:** three tables nothing uses are still in the schema, and `ai_usage` has no user id.

**Client structure (1.5 / 3)**
- The planner split is real: no file over 500 lines, and no runtime import cycle.
- But `VideoEditor` is still one function from line 236 to 2807, with 49 `useState` and 14 `useRef`.
- `MakeIt` is a 494-line component, and `overlays.ts` (991 lines) and `edit-command.ts` (872 lines) are large.

**Test architecture (2 / 3)**
- 679 unit tests, 142 e2e tests, 6 tests against real Postgres, one test per row of the allowance give-back rule, and a check that runs at type-check time.
- But e2e replaces every server action's result, CI runs with accounts switched off, and only 1 of the 7 paid actions runs through its real path.

**Docs accuracy and hygiene (2 / 3)**
- Every ARCHITECTURE.md claim I checked holds: limits, time limits, caps, where the 5-minute caps live, the Supabase services that are off, the boot check that exits, PostHog's settings (page views are sent by hand), the pages that refresh the session, and the type-only cycle.
- The PROGRESS_NOTES.md box is true.
- Template text still describes a deployment that doesn't exist.

### What still costs marks, most expensive first

1. **`VideoEditor` is one huge component.** (Size: large.)
   - **Where:** `src/app/[locale]/editor/video-editor.tsx:236–2807`.
   - **Why:** every editor change goes through one 2,572-line function holding 49 pieces of state.
   - **Smallest fix:** move state into hooks by concern (playback, edit history, assistant, projects), one PR each.
2. **`MakeIt` is the second god component.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `src/app/[locale]/(app)/make/make-it.tsx:129–622`, in a 1,162-line file.
   - **Smallest fix:** pull out the flow's state into hooks, as for the editor.
3. **Most paid actions are never tested through their real path.** (Size: medium.)
   - **Where:** `e2e/editor-helpers.ts:160–195` swaps in canned results; only `parseEditCommand` runs for real (`paid-action.db-test.mts`).
   - **Why:** six of the seven actions are proven only with everything mocked.
   - **Smallest fix:** add the other six to the database tests, each with the network faked at `fetch`.
4. **Two Supabase calls per paid action.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `src/proxy.ts:25–60` refreshes the session on `/editor` and `/make`, which includes the server action posts to those pages; then `requireUser` (`src/server/auth/session.ts:22`) checks again.
   - **Why:** extra latency on every AI call. It's behind the 4–5 s delays that made e2e flaky.
   - **Smallest fix:** skip the proxy's `getUser` for requests that carry a `next-action` header.
5. **Template docs describe a deployment that doesn't exist.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `docs/rules/deployment.md:7–12` says it auto-deploys to Vercel; `docs/CI_CD_SETUP.md` mentions Vercel 15 times; `CRON_SECRET` is declared at `src/lib/env.ts:54` and in `ci.yml`, but nothing reads it.
   - **Smallest fix:** reword those docs as not deployed, and drop the variable.
6. **Three unused tables.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `admin_members`, `feature_flags` and `feature_flag_overrides` (`prisma/schema/authz.prisma`, `flags.prisma`).
   - **Smallest fix:** a migration of their own that drops them.
7. **One sentence overclaims.** (Size: small.)
   - **Where:** `ARCHITECTURE.md:47–51`, "Only four things leave it".
   - **Why:** PostHog events and Sentry reports also leave the browser once their keys are set.
   - **Smallest fix:** add that clause.

### Fine, or deliberate trade-offs
- The single place for AI calls, with cost tracking.
- Export recorded in the browser, and projects kept in IndexedDB.
- The 5-minute caps, enforced on the server.
- The give-back rule counting timeouts as used, and saying so.
- The type-only import cycle, which is documented and gone at runtime.

### What wouldn't hold up if deployed
- **Request size:** a body limit of about 13.8 MB, against a host's 4.5 MB.
- **Duration:** actions run up to 60 s, with no `maxDuration` set.
- **Spend:** no ceiling across all accounts, and spend can't be traced to an account.
- **Sign-up:** no email confirmation or captcha.
- **Sign-in rate limits:** sign-in runs as a server action, so Supabase's per-IP limits see the server's address.
- **Projects** live in one browser.
- **Export** runs in real time, with the tab in front.
- **Migrations:** nothing would apply them to a hosted database.

**A stricter reviewer** would give about 9 (the god components, and e2e never running a real handler). **A kinder one** would give about 12 (docs that now match the code, and the cleanups behind PRs #51–55).

## 2. Claude logs: 10.5 / 15

| Log | Format | Prompts | Replies | Notifications | Summaries |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10-01 `62ad89d8` | old | 75 | 73 | 25 | 1 |
| 10-02 `bb3649b5` | old | 7 | 11 | 1 | 0 |
| 10-02 `e275341f` | old | 18 | 18 | 0 | 0 |
| 10-03 `de7ea219` | old | 30 | 30 | 1 | 2 |
| 10-04 `454b3fc1` | old | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 10-05 `819482d5` | old | 35 | 34 | 10 | 2 |
| 10-07 `90819801` | old | 81 | 78 | 34 | 3 |
| 10-10 `b42658ff` | **new** | 94 (all typed) | 986 | 0 real (1 is your own text) | 0 |

**How the format affects the score**
- **Only the newest log uses the new format.** It has no notifications, every reply in order, no email in the header, and a typed-only count.
- **It's also where most of the PR-based work is,** so it lifts the score.
- **The seven old logs still carry 71 notifications and 8 context summaries posing as prompts,** missing replies and your git email in every header. The 10-02 header says 3 exchanges while its body has 7 prompts.
- **The new log is complete but long:** 986 replies, many of them one-line progress notes. It's harder to skim.

| Area | Score | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Goal and plan for each task | 2 / 3 | Recent tasks have a GOAL, DONE MEANS and RULES; early sessions are loose |
| Decisions with reasons | 1.5 / 2 | Claude gives reasons; your own are rarely written down |
| Tests, PR and CI for each task | 2 / 3 | Full in the newest log (PR links, CI per job, test counts); none in the first four logs |
| You directing the work | 3.5 / 4 | Sharp catches ("you never read #54's CI"), proof demanded before changes, approval gates for test changes |
| Completeness and readability | 1.5 / 3 | Clean only in the newest log; the old seven are noisy; the new one is verbose |

Status: I've mapped the whole component, and here's the proposed split. Nothing in the repo has changed yet.

**Proposed hooks, safest to riskiest** (one hook per PR, in this order):

| # | Hook (new file under `editor/`) | Owns | Lines | Needs | Used by the rest | Effects it moves |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `useEditorShortcuts` | the keyboard handler and its effect | 19 | 3 (`undoEdit`, `redoEdit`, `showOriginal`) | nothing | 1 (`[]`, at line 1087) |
| 2 | `useMediaUrls` | revoking the object URLs of added and main videos, `releaseAddedUrls` | 17 | 2 | 1 | 2 (`[]` at 566, `[videoUrl]` at 574) |
| 3 | `useEditHistory` | `history`, `savedHistory`, record / undo / redo / reset, `unsaved` | 23 | 4 | 8 | 0 |
| 4 | `useCutEditor` | the filler-cut editor's state, window and 5 handlers | 39 | 9 | 8 | 0 |
| 5 | `useReRecord` | re-record's state, take and join previews, by-ear joins, 8 handlers | 117 | 14 | 14 | 0 |
| 6 | `usePlayerClock` | duration, current time, segment, picture size, playing, the metadata/time/play handlers | 49 | 5 | 16 | 0 |
| 7 | `useProjectFile` | the project, save status, relink, save / open / relink / remove, autosave | 122 | 27 | 12 | 1 (autosave, a 17-item dependency list) |
| 8 | `usePickedItem` | the picked item on the video, the text lane's items, retime / delete / replace / select | 147 | 19 | 10 | 0 |
| 9 | `useEditedTimeline` | derived values only: edited segments, overlays, segments, grade filters, the shown transform and zoom | 121 | 38 | 19 | 0 |
| 10 | `useAddItems` | adding a title, text, a mark, a logo, audio or video at the playhead, attaching files, seeking | 115 | 28 | 11 | 0 |
| 11 | `useEditDocument` (likely 2–3 PRs by sub-concern) | the edit's 21 state values, `snapshot`, `applySnapshot`, `restore` and 36 change handlers | 425 | 27 | 81 | 0 |
| 12 | `useAssistant` | Make it's hand-off, plans and follow-ups, highlights, `commandContext`, `applyCommand`, the start effect | 247 | **69** | 10 | 1 (`[]`, at line 1726) |
| 13 | the `tools` config, moved to a module of its own | the tool rail's 15 panels (render config) | 330 | **89** | 2 | 0 |

**What I'd leave in the component:**
- the hook calls and the glue between them;
- `video`, `analysis`, `edit`, `sequence`, `range`, `loadFile`;
- the UI-only state (`openTool`, `titleDraft`, `clipSelection`, `rotationDraft`, `showOriginal`, `previewRate`, `preview`);
- the JSX (529 lines).

**What's tangled, and how I'd handle it as pure moves:**
- **State that `segments` needs before `playback` exists** (the cut editor, re-record's three previews, the player clock, and the edit's state), while those concerns' handlers need `playback`. Each such hook file would export two functions: a state hook called where the state is declared now, and its actions called after `playback`. One concern per file, nothing reordered.
- **`preview` is shared** by the cut editor, re-record, the player clock and `segments`. It stays in the component and is passed to each.
- **`applyCommand` (123 lines) and `tools` (316 lines)** reach into almost every other concern (69 and 89 inputs). Moving them means passing everything in, so they're last and riskiest.

**Effect order is preserved.** React runs effects in the order they're declared, and hook calls count:
1. the built-in hooks' effects (`analysis`, `annotationTools`, `panelsShown`, `exporter`, `playback`), in that order;
2. `[]` at line 566;
3. `[videoUrl]` at 574;
4. autosave at 1021;
5. the shortcut handler at 1087;
6. start at 1726.

Every hook that takes an effect is called exactly where that effect is now, and every dependency array moves character for character.

**The under-800-lines target:**
- **Hooks 1–12** leave about 1,100 lines.
- **Moving the `tools` config (13)** brings it to about 760–800.
- **The JSX alone is 529 lines.** That's the floor without splitting the JSX into sub-components, which I'd treat as out of scope.

**Proof for each PR:** unit tests after every move, and the full e2e suite once per PR on my own `:3200` test server with invalid AI keys (fake AI only), as before; your `:3000` stays untouched.

The measurements change the picture, so I'll state the main finding first.

**Pure-move hooks alone can't get `video-editor.tsx` below about 1,600 lines,** even with all 12 remaining hooks done. Each hook adds a call that destructures what it returns and takes what it needs, and Prettier puts one name per line once a call passes 100 characters. Across the 12 hooks those calls add back about 575 lines. Under 1,500 needs one more pure move beyond the hooks. Under 800 isn't reachable by pure moves at all. These are estimates from the code's current shape, give or take about 10%.

**The 12 remaining hooks** (current main: 2,866 lines; effects at lines 567, 575, 1022 and 1708):

| Hook | Lines it would remove (net) | Risk | Must come before it | What it depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `useMediaUrls` | ~17 | low | nothing | `addedUrlsRef`, `videoUrl`; takes the effects at lines 567 and 575 |
| `useEditHistory` | ~10 | low | nothing (called after `snapshot`) | `snapshot`, `restore`, `video`, `hasDuration` |
| `useCutEditor` | ~19 | medium | nothing | `playback`, `cuts`, `range`, `recordEdit`, the shared `preview` setter; two calls, around `playback` |
| `useReRecord` | ~89 | medium | nothing | `playback`, `replacements`, `sequence`, `analysis`, `edit`, the shared `preview`; two calls |
| `usePlayerClock` | ~25 | medium | best after cut editor and re-record | `playback`, and the preview setters it clears; its state is read by almost everything before `playback` |
| `useProjectFile` | ~85 | medium | best after edit history | 27 values: `snapshot`, `applySnapshot`, `loadFile`, `playback`, the edit's 15 fields that autosave watches; takes the autosave effect (17 dependencies) |
| `usePickedItem` | ~119 | low–medium | nothing | 19 values: the text items, the change handlers it calls, `seekEdited`, `pick` |
| `useEditedTimeline` | ~56 | medium–high | best after cut editor, re-record, player clock | 38 values. It's all derived, but it feeds `playback`, so two calls around it |
| `useAddItems` | ~83 | medium | best after edit document | 28 values: the add and change handlers, the frame and transform values |
| `useEditDocument` | ~346 | **high** | best after everything above | 21 pieces of state, `snapshot`, `restore`, 36 handlers; read by everything (81 values out) |
| `useAssistant` | ~173 | **high** | best after edit document | 69 values: `applyCommand` calls about 30 of the editor's handlers; takes the start effect at line 1708 |
| `tools` config, moved out | ~243 | medium | nothing (render config only) | 89 values, the most of any |

**Too tangled to move safely as pure moves:**
- **`applyCommand` and `commandContext`.** They're the whole editor's API in one place. I'd leave them in the component and move only `useAssistant`'s Make it hand-off part: studio, plans, follow-ups and the start effect. That's about 100 of its 173 lines.
- **`useEditDocument` as a single hook.** It would return 81 values. It needs splitting by sub-concern (text and graphics; clips and audio; picture), and `snapshot`, `restore` and `applySnapshot` would still touch all three.

**Shortest safe route to below about 1,500.** Biggest safe savings first; the risky ones go last:

| PR | Move | Saves | `video-editor.tsx` after |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6.2 | `usePickedItem` | ~119 | ~2,750 |
| 6.3 | `useProjectFile` | ~85 | ~2,665 |
| 6.4 | `useReRecord` | ~89 | ~2,575 |
| 6.5 | the two helper components after `VideoEditor` (`VideoToolButton`, `VideosPanel`) and the 40 lines of helpers above it, moved to their own files | ~110 | ~2,465 |
| 6.6 | `tools` config | ~243 | ~2,220 |
| 6.7 | `useAddItems` | ~83 | ~2,140 |
| 6.8 | `useEditedTimeline` | ~56 | ~2,085 |
| 6.9–6.11 | `useEditDocument`, in 3 parts | ~346 | ~1,740 |
| 6.12 | `useAssistant`, hand-off part only | ~100 | ~1,640 |
| 6.13–6.16 | `useMediaUrls`, `useEditHistory`, `useCutEditor`, `usePlayerClock` | ~71 | ~1,570 |

That's 15 more PRs, two of them high-risk, and it still ends around 1,570. **Below about 1,500 isn't reliably reachable by pure moves.** Only the full assistant move, which I've just called too tangled to move safely, would get there.

**Below about 800 needs something other than pure moves.** What's left after every hook is the JSX (529 lines), imports and helpers (about 300), and the hook calls (about 575). Two changes would do it, and neither is a pure move:
- **One editor object passed around**, a context or a single `editor` value, instead of dozens of destructured names. That removes most of the ~575 lines of call. The trade-off: every use changes from `title` to `editor.title`.
- **Splitting the JSX into a few sub-components** (preview, timeline, side panel). That's a render refactor, which I'd treated as out of scope.

**My recommendation:**
- Do 6.2–6.6 now: five medium-or-lower PRs that bring the file to about 2,220, and cover the clearest concerns.
- Then decide between the risky `useEditDocument` and `useAssistant` moves and the editor-object approach, which would get to about 1,500 and below in fewer PRs but isn't line-for-line.

Should I start PR 6.2 (`usePickedItem`)? I'll wait for your yes.
